# Process And Staffing decisions

Team practices, experiments, staffing, review, and decision-making rules.
Product delivery and benchmark rules belong in their own area files.

## In force

- [D12](#d12) — Experiment E1 (true mobbing) stays open and opt-in behind a flag, not the default practice; its numbers are labelled early data. Follows D8
- [D15](#d15) — Tooling standard: a hardened smoke check before first use, non-author review of tooling, rotation authorship with logged takeovers
- [D18](#d18) — Process retro picks: thin slices with locked Done checks before coding, trialled on the C# adapter chain; metric is runs per card and cap hits
- [D22](#d22) — Atomic land-or-discard runs are an experiment (E3); dependency-free work may run in parallel, dependent or shared-state work stays at WIP of one
- [D27](#d27) — Stories name a persona, not a role, with an observable capability and a so-that value
- [D88](#d88) — Standing driver rule: the loop re-probes unavailable models and the driver status table is kept visible
- [R4](#r4) — Staffing is by role first and model second
- [D101](#d101) — Docs state principles and rationale, not who said them; no personal names, no verbatim quotes attributed to a role, no gendered pronouns about the sponsor
- [D109](#d109) — Decider seats are by role, not by model

Related decisions: [D5](board-and-delivery.md#d5), [D7](board-and-delivery.md#d7), [D23](board-and-delivery.md#d23), [D24](board-and-delivery.md#d24), [D28](board-and-delivery.md#d28), [D113](board-and-delivery.md#d113)

#### Decider D88: driver re-probing as a standing rule, the Copilot verdict timing, and the cheat-sheet-plus-index hypothesis

- Date: 2026-10-28. Inputs from product owner via Dispatch: query Copilot's quota and reset time; hold the verdict page until the reset unless it is more than about 48 hours away (then publish with Copilot unavailable and rerun later); a tracked re-probe; as a standing rule the loop always tests for unavailable models to be available again, built into the loop and the default template; and a hypothesis, not a directive: the core grammar and base library as a compact cheat sheet always in the prompt, extensions and adapters as an index of phrase signatures with full definitions pulled on demand, claim that prompt tokens stay roughly flat as the library grows, lookup tokens and round trips charged, pre-registered, not ahead of the prototype path.
- Facts: the GitHub account endpoint (gh api copilot_internal/user) reports Copilot premium interactions entitlement 1500, used 1504, has_quota false, quota_reset_date_utc 2026-11-01T00:00:00Z, about 88 hours from the probe (more than the 48 hour threshold); the CLI prints only You have no quota with a request id (no reset time) and its usage view exists only in the interactive UI; agy is out too: its CLI error text says the individual quota resets in 108h50m from the probe at about 08:50 UTC on 2026-10-28, which is about 2026-11-01 21:40 UTC.
- Seats: round 1 Codex No (card 139 belongs with mob-defaults process work and probes need bounded timeouts, failure logging and fake-probe tests) and Grok Support-it; round 2 both No (the files were not yet committed when they read the snapshot; Grok: card 139 must not sit inside the Usable v1 path fixed by D86 and card 140 must state it does not amend D32). Both points were applied: files committed, card 139 ranked with the other loop cards below the ZOMBIES card 134, card 140 states that it is a separate question that does not amend D32 or rescore stored rows. Round 3: Codex No (agy was not being re-probed), Grok no vote; applied: agy probed, DRIVERS.md updated, a tracked 60 minute agy re-probe started. Round 4: Codex No (this record stale and incomplete) and Grok No (the agy reset arithmetic was eight hours late, no flag file named, the record still said about 1 November); applied in this record and in DRIVERS.md. Round 5: Codex No and Grok No (decision (a) named only Copilot's reset, agy-back.flag unnamed, no final votes recorded); applied here. Result: see the final line.
- Decision: (a) the holdout verdict is published INCONCLUSIVE by the frozen bar (fewer than 3 models, Copilot's holdout rows unavailable) naming BOTH unavailable drivers and their resets (Copilot 2026-11-01 00:00 UTC from the account endpoint; agy about 2026-11-01 21:40 UTC from its error text), and is regenerated from the same frozen numbers after the unavailable rows are rerun: Copilot's holdout rows are rerun when it returns; agy ran no holdout rows (it was never an included model of this run), so its return restores its seats and adds rows only if product owner asks; a probe that succeeds before publication runs Copilot's holdout rows first; Codex and Grok rows stay valid. A tracked 30 minute re-probe of the account endpoint runs now (no quota used) and writes copilot-back.flag when quota returns; a tracked 60 minute agy re-probe writes agy-back.flag when it answers (both in the facilitator scratch folder, named in DRIVERS.md); the flag completions wake the facilitator. (b) The Driver availability rule is in TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md and the mob-defaults default template; DRIVERS.md is the visible status table and event log (MOB.md is generated); templates/DRIVERS.default.md is added to mob-defaults. (c) Card 139: the loop step with fake-probe tests, ranked after card 134 with the loop cards. (d) Card 140: the hybrid prompt measurement, pre-registered, ranked after card 139.
- Final: round 6: Codex Yes, Grok Yes (the record, both resets, the ranks 051 at 47, 134 at 55, 139 at 56 and 140 at 57 verified from the committed files). Result: passed.

<a id="r4"></a>
Record R4 (the product owner's standing rule, 2026-10-28, folded in as instructed; not a vote): staffing is by ROLE first and by model second (Staffing: roles over models in TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md and the mob-defaults template). Roles are the primary perspectives; when a model is out of quota its roles move to available models; Decider votes and reviews are seated by role, so an outage does not make a vote smaller; one model may fill several roles only in separate fresh contexts; Claude (Sonnet by default) is a normal member of the rotation and takes seats while Copilot is out, but is the sole driver only when every other driver is out; model diversity is preferred but never blocks. The headless claude CLI is not logged in (checked 2026-10-28: Not logged in), so Claude is seated as a fresh-context subagent given the role charter and the proposal. From now on this repository's reviews seat the role of the missing Copilot reviewer with Claude.

<a id="d109"></a>
#### Decider D109: Decider seats are by role, and decisions stay reversible

- Date: 2026-10-28. Question: how is the Decider seated when a model runs out of credits, and what keeps a wrong call cheap now that fewer models review. Decided as a standing principle by the product owner (no design dispute); recorded here and in the working agreement and the mob-defaults template. Result: passed.
- Decision: (1) Decider seats are by role, not by model. Every role is seated; each seat is filled by an available model in a fresh context; one model may fill several role-seats (never reviewing its own authorship); a vote is never taken with fewer seats because a model is out; the decision record names which model filled which role-seat. Reason: the diverse perspectives come from the roles, and a shrinking vote quietly weakens every decision. (2) Decisions stay reversible: each card, commit and decision is small enough to revert cleanly on its own; a decision that cannot be undone cheaply is flagged as a one-way door and escalated as the Escalation section says, otherwise it is split until it can be undone; the record names the revert step. Reason: with fewer models reviewing a wrong call is more likely, and cheap reversal is the safety net.
- Effect now: Grok is out (usage balance exhausted); the roles it filled are filled by Codex and fresh-context Claude seats. D100 to D108 listed the seats that voted; from D110 each record lists the role, the model that filled it and the context (fresh or not).

#### Decider D116: retro experiment E3 (the decision log split by area) starts its trial; review fixes applied

- Date: 2026-10-29. Question: does the split by area (five area files, index, mapping, migration proof) hold on commit 6c5e0f4 and fixes; may the trial start. Seats by role (D109): reviewer, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: Yes with fixes (the stub carries no decision numbers so references of the form DECIDERS.md D86 land on a page without them; two stale line citations in the extension map ADR; D31 recorded as superseded by D86 though D86 never names it; D82 recorded as fully superseded though D84 replaces only the real-project tier; a naming collision with the earlier use of E3 in D22); the facilitator found and fixed an earlier defect (a sign-off section with no decision id fell between two records and was lost; the recorded hash is now the sha256 of the original log's records, computed from git, and two regression tests cover it) and ran the full Python suite (211 tests). Result: passed.
- Applied: the stub now says to find a decision by number in INDEX.md; the extension map ADR cites the area file and record number; D31 is recorded as applied (its priority later changed by D86) and D82 as in force (its real-project tier was replaced by D84, the synthetic tier stays); E3 carries a naming note. Verified independently by the reviewer: 106 records, each in exactly one file, the region hash equals the recorded value, the area files reproduce the original record text apart from added link anchors.
- Trial: until the next retro, measure the tokens a fresh seat loads for one area plus the index (for example compiler seat: index 20 KB plus 37 KB, against 143 KB before). New records go to their area file with one areas.json entry (this record and D115 are the first). The Decider keeps, revises or drops E3 at the retro; the mob-defaults template keeps the single-file form until then.
- Reversal: one commit adds the split; reverting it restores the single file (the original is in history).

<a id="d118"></a>
#### Decider D118: the stale-CLI test helper fix is its own card, ranked next (card 173)

- Date: 2026-10-29. Question: a stale phrase CLI binary gave a false red in card 170 and can give a false green, because tests trust whatever phrase.dll is on disk; pull the fix out of card 172 and rank it next, or leave it in 172, or add a rule only. Options: A its own small card ranked ahead of 169 and 171 (the helper fails loudly when any CLI-relevant source is newer than the dll); B inside 172 after 171 with an interim rebuild rule; C a rule only. Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex, fresh context: Support-it for A (a small reversible safeguard against costly false greens in every incremental local gate); test-quality reviewer, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: A (34 calls in seven test files trust the binary on disk, including all the trace and source-map tests; fail loudly rather than rebuild in the helper, because a second test process would race on the shared build folders; compare the newest source write time to the dll with a small tolerance; prove it with injected timestamps; risk: a false stale failure after a touch, so the message must say what to run); delivery lead, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: B (CI builds clean and the facilitator rebuilds before every gate, so exposure is narrow; 172 touches the same helper file; risk: a false green before 172 lands). Grok is out of balance. Votes 2 to 1 for A. Result: passed.
- Decision: A. Card 173 holds the one fix and is ranked first in the queue, ahead of 169 and 171, with the helper failing loudly (not rebuilding) and one injectable-timestamp test. Interim rule until it lands: every local run rebuilds src/Phrase.Cli first (already the facilitator's practice before each gate; the dissent is recorded as the reason the rule stays). Card 172 keeps the shared process helper and the trace hardening; it no longer carries the stale-binary check.
- Reversal: the card is additive test-support code; reverting it restores the existence-only check.

<a id="d120"></a>
#### Decider D120: the edge-case fixture corpus is card 174, ranked right after 173; the rest is card 175

- Date: 2026-10-29. Question: where to rank the edge-case fixture corpus (small Phrase files, one tricky shape each, expected outcomes pinned, one iterating test run by every compiler and adapter change), and whether the card is the right size. Context: two regressions this week passed every test because the Tasks and Orders fixtures have no nested phrases or shared failures, and reviewers caught them by reading diffs. Options: A right after 173, ahead of 169, 171, 172, 165 and 166; B after 169 and 171; C after 165. Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex, fresh context: Support-it for A (the corpus should land before 171, 165 and 166, which touch adapter and emitter behaviour it protects; delaying the independent 169 is a small reversible cost); delivery lead, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: A (165, 166, 169 and 171 would otherwise be reviewed by reading diffs; it follows 173 naturally; risk: writing the outcomes may expose existing bugs and stall the card; this seat could not read the proposal file, which my background command had not yet written, and voted from the card and the dependencies); test-quality reviewer, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: A (the inline compiler tests already cover cycles, unknown phrases and composite ordering, but not failures inherited through composites, a failure shared by endpoints, two declarers, CRLF or the source-map records of those shapes; criterion 3 only works if a fixture asserts the source-map record of the inherited failure; risk: outcomes regenerated from current output; also asked to split the card). Grok is out of balance. Votes 3 to 0 for A. Result: passed.
- Decision: A with the test-quality seat's changes. Card 174 is ranked right after 173 and holds the iterating test plus the shapes card 170 and the earlier regressions touched (a shared failure, a multi-level composite, a composite calling a failing phrase, two declarers, an earlier step raising the same name, CRLF, a native-only step); its expected outcomes are written by hand and never regenerated from current output, every fixture whose bug would show in the map pins the map records, and reverting card 170's fix must turn the inherited-failure fixture red. Shapes the inline tests already cover (a call cycle, an unknown phrase) are not duplicated. Card 175 holds the remaining Zero, Boundary and Exception shapes (no endpoints, an empty record, the longest name, an undeclared native failure) and depends on 174. Queue after 173: 174, 175, then 169, 171, 172, 165, 166, 162.
- Reversal: adding fixtures and one iterating test is additive; reverting removes the corpus and returns reviewers to reading diffs.

<a id="d121"></a>
#### Decider D121: the v0.1 public preview bar (docs/release/v0.1-bar.md) is accepted with named changes; the gap cards are filed and ranked

- Date: 2026-10-29. Question: is the draft v0.1 bar (persona Dana; eight observable bars; a gap table with sizes; an estimate; the one-way doors) the smallest honestly releasable preview. Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex, fresh context: Support-it with three changes (state and test the runtime prerequisites; actionable messages on the getting-started path, broader diagnostic polish after the preview; report effort and wall-clock separately, because 12 S, 8 M and 1 L imply about 52 to 104 effort-hours; bar 5 belongs in the preview, with one measurement per criterion, limits and unfavourable results published); product manager and delivery lead, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: Support-it with changes (the counts did not add up: restate as about 20 required cards; relax bar 3 to a stated reason per native block and move the large store-operations card out of the gate; gate bar 5 on the honest verdict page plus one published first measurement, with 144 optional; size 149 to 151 as M to L; add the one-way doors the draft missed: the name, dependency licences, vendor terms for publishing benchmark outputs, the repository history, a stability expectation); outside developer Dana, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: No with named changes (a one-screen README, prerequisites with a bash-free Windows path, what to do after generating, an actionable-failure bar, an issues route; defer the native-block limit; shrink bar 6 to the existing corpus; make bar 5 lighter). Grok is out of balance. Votes 2 Support-it to 1 No. Result: passed.

The architect's wish for one measurement per criterion and the outside developer's wish for a lighter gate are reconciled by gating on one and publishing the others as they land, labelled. Counts restated: 19 required cards (6 existing, 13 new); effort 40 to 70 hours; wall-clock 4 to 8 working days as a target that needs parallel work and quick answers to the one-way doors.
- Cards filed (D121): 176 package the CLI as a dotnet tool (M), 177 CI install check on Windows and Linux (S), 178 prerequisites page tested against the CI matrix (S), 179 README rewrite and getting-started guide (M), 180 guide drift test (S), 181 a cold model completes the guide (S), 182 actionable messages on the guide path (M), 183 licence file (S, one-way door), 184 version and changelog (S), 185 issues route, contributing and security note (S), 186 pre-publication checks (S), 187 known-limitations page (S), 188 publish the first version (S, one-way door). Ranked: 183, 186, 176, 178, 179 right after 175 (the owner questions and the independent lane start now, beside the compiler cards); 177, 180, 181, 182, 184, 185, 187, 188 after 162.
- One-way doors for the product owner: the licence, the public package name and owner and the first publish, the first public claims, and the results of the pre-publication checks (name and trademark, dependency licences, vendor terms, history and secrets). Nothing is published without written approval.

<a id="d124"></a>
#### Decider D124: review of card 183 (licence files) and the product owner's licence decision

- Date: 2026-10-29. Decision recorded from the product owner (a one-way door, decided by the product owner and not by the mob): Apache License 2.0 plus a trademark policy. Code may be modified and reused under Apache-2.0 with attribution kept through the NOTICE file; modified versions may not use the project's name or logo; code generated by the compiler belongs to the user with no license obligations from the toolchain; the copyright holder stays a clearly marked placeholder until the product owner confirms it at the pre-publication checks (card 186); nothing is published or renamed. Rationale in the documents: the name signals the official, accountable toolchain.
- Review of the prepared files (commits 39ef640 and 31c6f5d). Seats by role (D109): reviewer, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context (not a lawyer; checks consistency and traps): Yes with fixes (the trademark-risk text had landed on card 187 because three issue numbers are offset from their card numbers; NOTICE carried policy sentences that a NOTICE must not carry; the trademark wording needed a rule for suffixed names and unofficial labelling; mixed spellings), all applied. Result: passed.
- Evidence: LICENSE is the standard Apache-2.0 text (11,358 bytes, sha256 cfc7749b96f63bd31c3c42b5c471bf756814053e847c10f3eb003417bc523d30, checked by a test; pinned to LF in .gitattributes); NOTICE is attribution only with the marked holder placeholder; TRADEMARKS.md and docs/LICENSING.md state unmodified redistribution may use the name, forks must rename (including suffixed names), factual references are fine, and generated code belongs to the user; the README carries the same statement; five tests in tests/test_license_files.py. Card 186 carries the existing software brand named Phrase as a risk (a trademark search and the product owner's confirmation of the public name before the first publish) and four questions for a lawyer (the generated runtime helper files and the ownership statement, enforceability of a trademark policy on this name, the rename rule against Apache-2.0 notice retention, the holder).
- Open: the holder line, the public name and a trademark search (card 186), the helper-file question (a lawyer), and the issue-number offset on cards 186 to 188 (issue 186 is card 187, issue 187 is card 188, issue 188 is card 186).
- Reversal: the files are additive documents; the choice of licence itself cannot be taken back for versions already published, which is why nothing is published before card 186.

<a id="d125"></a>
#### Decider D125: the public name is Avowry; the rename as nine small cards

- Date: 2026-10-29. Decision recorded from the product owner (a one-way door, decided by the product owner and not by the mob): the public name is Avowry, replacing Phrase as the product, language, CLI and package name, because Phrase collides with the Phrase localization-platform brand. The word phrase stays inside the language as a descriptive term for one line of intent. A preliminary screen found the NuGet id, npm, PyPI, crates.io, the GitHub handle, avowry.dev and avowry.io free; a professional trademark search stays on card 186 before the first publish. Nothing is published.
- Question to the Decider: the source file extension, how long to read the old one, and the order of the cards. Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex: A (.avowry); the old extension is read with a deprecation warning until 1.0; the order is sound, but the internal names (cards 193 to 196) land before the first publish because assembly and metadata names are externally observable; repository rename last. Delivery lead, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: A; read .phrase with a warning through 0.x and remove it at 1.0, add a migrate command; the internal renames must land before the first publish (they ship inside the package and in users' generated code); split the project renames per project; the user-facing documents card is safe before the card 162 re-freeze except that tests/test_license_files.py asserts exact wording and must change in the same card, and the hash-frozen GRAMMAR.md and CHEATSHEET.md stay out of it. Language and developer-experience reviewer, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: A; .avowry is unambiguous and unique while .av and .avw collide with other file types; read .phrase until 1.0 then fail with a message naming the migrate command; refuse to guess when both spellings exist; one sentence explains the unit ("Avowry is the language; each phrase is one line of intent in it"); one command spelling, no short alias for now. Result: passed, unanimous on all three questions.
- Plan (cards 190 to 198): 190 user-facing documents and legal files; 191 command avowry, tool output and the map file name; 192 the extension .avowry, .phrase read with a warning until 1.0, and avowry migrate; 193 names in generated code (after cards 166 and 162, with the re-freeze); 194 compiler project; 195 adapter and CLI projects; 196 test projects; 197 solution, CI, scripts and paths; 198 repository rename, last. Amended: 176 (command avowry, package id Avowry), 162 (the re-freeze carries the Avowry wording and the new generated names), 179 (the guide uses the avowry command and .avowry files), 186 (the trademark search is on Avowry, with domains and the holder). Each rename card is gated by a grep audit that lists the remaining hits with a reason.
- Risks recorded: old names left in anything a model reads (the cheat sheet, prompts, error messages) keep teaching .phrase, so each card audits by grep; path-bound tests (the demo README test, the Usable v1 hashes, the CI checks) turn red unless changed in the same card; the frozen documents change only in the card 162 re-freeze, with an LLM-check rerun.
- Reversal: each card is a small, separately revertable rename; the first publish (card 188) is the point after which names cannot be taken back, which is why cards 190 to 197 come before it and card 198 (the repository, with redirects) is last.

<a id="d127"></a>
#### Decider D127: no backward compatibility for the rename; the repository is renamed first

- Date: 2026-10-29. Decisions recorded from the product owner (not the mob): there are no users yet, so nothing keeps the old names alive (no .phrase reading, no phrase keyword with a warning, no migrate command, no severity field), the repository's own sources switch directly to .avowry and avow, the rename goes to the top of the board straight after card 175, nothing is published, and the commits stay small and green with no shims beyond what keeps each commit green. Rationale: a compatibility layer has a real cost and no beneficiary while there are zero users. Second decision from the product owner: the repository is renamed to avowry (same organisation, history, issues, board and CI) as soon as card 175 lands, before the other rename cards, because GitHub redirects the old addresses and there is no reason to wait; card 198 becomes that step, and the local folder keeps its name so running sessions and tools keep working.
- Sequencing check, seats by role (D109): architect, Codex: No unless the frozen documents, the orders, the hash assertions, the benchmark prompts and the scripts move in the same card as the keyword, and the sealed holdouts are renamed before unsealing with new hashes. Delivery lead, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: Yes with changes, the same two points: the tests that read the frozen documents and orders (the cheat sheet compile test, the Usable v1 hash binding, the benchmark and LLM-check scripts and their tests) would break between the keyword and the re-freeze, so the keyword, the extension, GRAMMAR.md, CHEATSHEET.md and the orders move in one mechanical commit, with the one hash-binding test marked pending (with its reason) until the LLM check is rerun; the extension and keyword cards merge because they touch the same files twice. The facilitator accepted both changes, so the formal result of the first reading (failed) became passed on the amended plan.
- Plan: after card 175, card 198 (repository rename, the local remote, links, scripts and CI that name the repository, CI green under the new name), then 190 (user-facing documents), 191 (command, output and map file name), 199 (the keyword avow with fillers, the .avowry extension, the documents, the orders, all sources: one mechanical commit; card 192 is merged into it), 193 (generated-code names), 194 to 197 (internal renames), 162 (the single final re-freeze: fold the pattern steps, rerun the Usable v1 bar 6 LLM check, un-pend the hash-binding test, ratify the hashes), and card 201 (the sealed holdouts live outside the repository: the product owner converts them, new hashes are recorded before any run, and earlier benchmark rows are kept as a separate baseline, never compared with new ones). Card 200 (avowry fmt) stays after the release lane.
- Risks recorded: the one pending hash-binding test is a visible gap until the card 162 rerun; benchmark results before and after the keyword change are not comparable; the repository rename changes every shared link (redirects cover the old ones).
- Reversal: each card is a small revert; the repository rename can be reversed with a second rename (the old address redirects while the name is held); nothing is published.

<a id="d134"></a>
#### Decider D134: mob-process accountability that does not depend on the facilitator (after the 2026-10-26 to 2026-10-29 lapse)

- Date: 2026-10-29. Background: from 2026-10-26 16:36 (card 063) the mob ran cards without card mode, without a refreshed MOB.md and without a retro per Done card, and the only detector (card mode checks that a task force is declared) never ran because card mode was skipped. Product owner direction (fixed): the checks run outside facilitator.py, in CI on every push to main and as a required check, so skipping the process turns main red where everyone can see it; at minimum MOB.md is fresh, every Done card has a retro record and every Done card has a role-rotation trail (ready, slice, red, green, ship, signoff seats with model names) in .mob/; the records are machine-readable so an external auditor (Dispatch, every 30 minutes) can verify them from GitHub alone.
- Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex: Yes with counters (distinct evidence per role, freshness from git time, a workflow on issue events, an explicit issue-to-card mapping, seats checked against DRIVERS.md eligibility, a frozen legacy waiver). Independent auditor trying to defeat the process, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: No on the first design (the checker, records and waiver all live in files the facilitator edits) and No again on the amended design with one remaining hole: the records are self-reported, so a falsified record passes every check; the only non-forgeable counter is an external artifact (a review or status from a separate auditor identity, or commits signed by a seat-specific key). Result: passed with the auditor's dissent recorded; the residual hole (self-reported records) is accepted by the product owner ruling in D135, with the external audit as the control. The facilitator does not count the amended design as closing the hole.
- Design in force: one script scripts/mob_process.py with check (read-only, exit 1, one line per violation) and write-mob-md (the single, deterministic generator of the root MOB.md, which card mode calls). A Done card is a closed issue with the label card whose title carries the card number; every such card needs (a) a trail record .mob/records/NNN.json (schema 1) with seats for ready, slice, red, green, ship and signoff, each with role, seat, model and its OWN evidence (a commit on main whose message contains Card NNN, or a Decider id present in docs/decisions), seats listed as eligible in DRIVERS.md, the signoff seat different from the green seat, and the record first committed before the issue's closed_at; (b) a retro: a retro file whose Cards line lists the card; (c) a place in MOB.md (its done list equals the set of records). Legacy waiver: .mob/records/LEGACY.json lists the 91 cards closed before the restoration (sha256 of the list: 2618ef6c69243938dfad116f9f99e772d8a48dd3256b264a1a098ec1d42788d1, pinned here and checked), may not contain a card closed after its cutoff, and every legacy card must appear in the catch-up retro. An unlabeled event on a card issue fails; a reopened event needs a note in the record; a closed issue without the card label fails unless it carries not-card. MOB.md fresh means its as_of commit is an ancestor of HEAD and at most 3 non-MOB commits behind (no wall-clock rule) and regenerating it gives the same page. CI: a job mob-process in ci.yml on every push to main (fetch-depth 0), the pre-push hook, and a second workflow on issues closed, reopened and unlabeled plus a 30-minute schedule; the job fails if the workflow lacks the mob-process job. Records are written by card mode and committed by the shipper seat before the issue is closed. Tooling is authored by a rotation model test-first, each check shown failing by mutation, reviewed by non-authors.
- Owner actions: none. The first design asked for branch protection with required checks, a code-owner review by a second identity and an auditor identity; the product owner ruled (D135) that the mob stays trunk-based with direct commits and feature flags for unfinished work, so enforcement is detection (CI red, stop-the-line) plus the external audit, and those requests are withdrawn.
- Risks recorded: a card closed after the last push is caught only by the issue-event workflow or the schedule; a deterministic MOB.md can still be generated from falsified records; the legacy list was generated from GitHub at the cutoff and cannot grow.
- Reversal: the checker and records are additive files; removing the CI job turns the detector off, which is why the job's presence is itself checked and why branch protection is requested.

<a id="d135"></a>
#### Decider D135: review of the mob-process checker (rounds 1 to 3) and prevention versus detection

- Date: 2026-10-29. Part 1, prevention versus detection (an independent auditor found that main has no branch protection, so the CI check detects a skipped process after the push but cannot prevent it; the project pushes straight to main, and a required check would block every direct push because a push cannot carry a passing check yet). Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex: C. Independent auditor, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: C (it also read the repository settings read-only: main is not protected, there are no rulesets, force pushes and direct pushes are allowed). Result of the vote: passed, unanimous for C; superseded in part by the product owner ruling below. Product owner ruling after the vote (2026-10-29): the mob stays trunk-based with direct commits to main; there is no move to pull requests, no branch protection and no reviewer-identity request. Unfinished or risky work ships behind default-off feature flags (or an equivalent toggle) so that main stays deployable. Decision: detection plus the external audit is the STANDING design, not an interim step. It consists of a required job in ci.yml on every push, a workflow on issue events and every 30 minutes, red main with the stop-the-line rule (nothing else is started while main is red), the previous version of the checker also passing on the new tree, and an external audit by Dispatch every 30 minutes against its own pinned copy of the checker, reported to the product owner. What detection catches after the fact: a card closed with no record, a skipped retro, a stale MOB.md, a weakened checker (via the previous-version run), a hook-skipping push. What it does not catch by itself: a disabled CI job (only the external audit sees the absence), and a force push or history rewrite (the checker reads the rewritten history); the audit and the stop-the-line rule are the controls for those. Owner actions: none (the earlier requests for branch protection, a code-owner reviewer identity and an auditor identity are withdrawn). The earlier vote for option C (prevention as the destination) is superseded by this ruling; the votes are kept above as history.
- Part 2, review of the checker. Round 1 (commit 1aec553, Codex): fresh-context adversarial reviewer, Claude (Sonnet): Yes with fixes (tests missing for most paths, loose matching of Card N, wide Cards ranges, stub retros, edits to the checker judged only by itself). Round 2 (Codex, unpushed): the same reviewer: No (the previous-checker CI step had no token, it compared with the parent commit instead of the commit before the push, a legitimate generator change would fail the byte-identical MOB.md comparison, twelve tests were merged into eleven and coverage was lost, one test used the real clock). Round 3 (Codex): a new fresh-context reviewer found a wrong repository root in the new C# test and a clock-dependent test date; the facilitator fixed both (TAKEOVER, small, listed below) and the reviewer's other round-2 items were verified fixed (token on every checker step, github.event.before with safe skips, --skip-regeneration, no test names removed: 35 tests, 23 added, a MOB.md line-ending rule, sorted outputs). Result: passed after fixes. The author seat (Codex) did not vote on its own work.
- Auditor findings folded in after round 3: (1) prevention versus detection: Part 1. (2) The 91 exempt cards could be extended quietly: the sha256 of the frozen list is now pinned in the checker source (FROZEN_LEGACY_SHA256) as well as in D134, so extending LEGACY.json also needs a change to the checker, which the previous checker version (run by CI on every push to main) rejects; a test shows an extended list with a consistent hash and decision text still fails. (3) Cards 190, 191, 193, 194, 198 and 199 and the rest of the 91 have no per-card retro and no rotation trail; each is covered only by the catch-up retro Cards line, as a one-off. From card 195 on every Done card needs both a trail record and a retro, and the checker fails otherwise. (4) The trail is machine-readable: one .mob/records/NNN.json per Done card lists the seats ready, slice, red, green, ship and signoff, plus an optional refactor seat, each with the seat, the model name and its own evidence (a commit whose message names the card, or a Decider id that mentions it); empty seat, model or evidence fields and duplicate roles are rejected. The facilitator tags a commit with an authorship line, but only the seats in the record are audited.
- TAKEOVER log (facilitator edits to tooling normally authored by the rotation, each small and test-first): added test_closed_card_without_retro_cover_is_reported after finding that emptying check_retros survived; fixed the repository root in tests/Phrase.Acceptance.Tests/MobProcessAccountability.cs; moved the test closure date to 2099; added the legacy pin, the optional refactor seat and the empty-field check with their tests (each shown red by mutation).
- Residual risks (the reviewers' list, kept honest): records, retros and Cards lines are self-attested until an auditor identity posts an external review or status; seat distinctness is a name check; commit dates can be forged; the checker, workflows and tests can be edited in one commit, and only branch protection with code-owner review (and the external audit) protects them; the previous-version run happens after the push (detects, does not prevent); API rate limits or outages could turn main red without a process failure; the 30-minute schedule is the longest a violation can sit unseen by CI.
- Addendum, final state (written after the first rollout of round 3 and the audit of 104410f): (a) main went red at 104410f because the previous-version step ran the 1aec553 checker with --root and --skip-regeneration, which that version does not have; this was a one-time bootstrap failure; fixed in 171a731 (the step skips a previous checker that lacks the flag, with a notice, and a test pins the guard); on 171a731 the step ran the 104410f checker against the new tree and printed OK, which confirms the step works once both versions have the flags. (b) The test counts: 35 tests at the end of round 3, 37 after the legacy pin, the optional refactor seat and the empty-field check, 38 with the CI guard test, 42 at the final state (none removed from the original 12 names beyond the round 2 merge, which round 3 restored by adding tests). (c) Final-state review, fresh context, not the author and not a reviewer of the earlier rounds: Claude Sonnet 5.5 (claude-sonnet-5-5), verdict Yes on 171a731 plus the delta. Its first pass found that a null, numeric or list model passed the empty-field check, that malformed seats raised exceptions instead of reporting, and that two of the facilitator's own additions were not covered by tests (the retro cover line and the unknown-role clause); all three were fixed with new tests (each shown red against its mutant), and the wrongly typed fields now fail the run closed (TypeError, AttributeError and KeyError are caught and reported). The reviewer re-reviewed that delta and gave the final Yes. (d) The final changes made by the facilitator (TAKEOVER) were reviewed by that reviewer, so no change to the checker is unreviewed.
- Residual risks added (kept for the retro log and docs/PROCESS-CHECKS.md): (1) a single commit can make the checker permissive and still pass, because the previous checker validates the tree, not the checker source; the previous-version run is skipped on force pushes and on a boundary that is not an ancestor; only the external audit covers this (standing design, D135 ruling); (2) the previous-version run skips the byte comparison of MOB.md, depends on the live GitHub state, and its flag guard only checks that the string appears in the old file; (3) the older empty-field check in check_records is now dead code (harmless); (4) a seat with a missing key or a non-string role reports the empty-field message, not the six-role message (cosmetic); (5) records, retros and Cards lines remain self-attested; this is accepted as part of the standing design, with the external audit and the stop-the-line rule as the controls.
- Reversal: the checker, records and workflows are additive files; removing the job turns detection off, which the job-presence check and the external audit are meant to notice.

<a id="d136"></a>
#### Decider D136: thin slices, numeric limits, feature flags in a compiler repo, and retro timing (rules are forward-only from card 195)

- Date: 2026-10-29. Trigger: the product owner asked why card 195 takes so long when slicing is meant to be thin, and the independent auditor needs pass or fail rules instead of FLAG for slicing, flags and retro timing.
- Part 1, why so long, and the levers. Evidence from the card 195 card-mode run (events.jsonl, first 47 minutes): role seats plus failover about 1 minute (PM ready 16.6 s, architect slice 16.5 s, probing three unavailable seats 25.5 s), the change itself about 16 minutes (QA red 50.5 s, green 14 min 16 s, refactor 70 s) and two full test gates 11 min 3 s and 11 min 16 s (about 47 percent); the architect answered thin vertical slice in 16 s for a card with five increments because its ask was Confirm the card is a thin vertical slice, which invites yes; the card was also cut too large by the facilitator (two project renames, a schema change, CI, scripts and a report) and was widened in 4aa0ad7. CI for a green card takes 9 to 12 minutes; the earlier mechanical cards were big (193: 30 files, 194: 87 files, 199: 122 files). Seats: architect, Codex: Yes; delivery lead, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: Yes with changes. Decision: lever 1 smaller cards (the architect ask becomes decide SHIP AS IS or SPLIT and must output the split list; the PM also rejects oversized cards at ready time); lever 2 cut gate time without cutting seats (filtered tests after red and green, the full gate once at ship, find and fix the slowest acceptance tests); lever 3 skip seats marked UNAVAILABLE in DRIVERS.md (saves about 25 s, last); lever 4 measure commit-to-live per card in every retro. Process seats are not the lever. Card 195 is finished as is and is the baseline; the split rule applies from the next card.
- Part 2, numeric limits and exceptions (forward-only from card 195). A countable commit has a subject starting with Card NNN; countable files exclude board/, docs/decisions/, MOB.md, .mob/records/, docs/retros/ and generated expected.json fixtures. Limits per commit: at most 15 countable files and 400 changed lines (data: median 4.5 files and 148 lines, 75th percentile 14 files and 329 lines over the 16 card commits since 2026-10-28); review-fix commits (Card NNN review fixes) are separate commits under the same limits. Per card: at most three acceptance criteria (scored from the issue body), at most three increments and one concern, recorded by the architect in the trail record field slice (decision ship-as-is or split, files estimate, increments, concern in one line); the audit scores that the field exists and is within the limits and FLAGS only the judgment of one concern. Exceptions are one message line Slice-exception: KIND with KIND rename (git mv plus renamed paths; FAIL if fewer than 50 percent of the changed paths are renames by git diff -M, otherwise WARN with a count), rewrite (an identifier or keyword rewritten in place; the line must name its substitution, for example Slice-exception: rewrite s/Phrase/Avowry/, and the audit verifies that every changed line, apart from one pinning test file, is explained by that substitution; otherwise FAIL) or generated (all paths match the published list of generated-file patterns; otherwise FAIL). No hash condition is used (card 199 changed output). Commit-to-live: the trail record gets started_at (ISO, from the first card-mode turn event); commit-to-live is the completion time of the green CI run of the card last commit minus started_at; targets median 45 minutes and 90th percentile 90 minutes over the last five cards, reported as WARN data, never FAIL, never a quota; started_at is self-reported, so the audit cross-checks it against the first role commit push time and the issue In Progress time and FLAGS any started_at later than the first commit.
- Part 3, feature flags in a compiler and CLI repo. The product owner ruling stands (trunk-based; unfinished or risky work ships behind default-off flags). The agreement sentence Feature-flag policy: none is replaced. A flag is a named default-off feature registered in docs/FLAGS.md (name, owner card, what off means, the removal card or condition). The CLI reads AVOWRY_FEATURES (a comma list) or the option --enable NAME through one FeatureFlags class. Incomplete user-visible behaviour (new syntax, a new command or an output change, a risky generator change) must be behind a flag or be unreachable (not wired into any entry point). One test proves that off leaves the output unchanged and one proves on. Atomic renames, refactors and documents need no flag. Status=Pending traits stay for unfinished tests (see D137). Scoring: PASS when every flag name found in code is in docs/FLAGS.md with an open owner card, an off test and an on test, and no flag of a Done card remains; whether an unflagged change was user-visible and incomplete is FLAGGED for human judgment, never failed. The checks start when docs/FLAGS.md exists (the first flagged card creates it).
- Part 4, retro timing. Forward-only from card 195: the retro for card N (a retro file whose Cards line lists N) must be pushed before issue N is closed and before any later commit that names a different card (WIP is one card); the evidence is the push or CI-run time and the issue closedAt, never the commit date alone; commits that name card N itself (review fixes, CI fixes) are exempt; the catch-up retro covers the 91 legacy cards and is not scored retroactively (card 190 closed before this rule).
- Votes on parts 2 to 4, seats by role (D109): architect, Codex: No (round 1: hash condition for exceptions, timing from GitHub timestamps, concurrent cards), No (round 2: rewrites must pass independent of rename share), Yes (round 3, the three-kind rule). Independent auditor lens, Claude Sonnet (fresh context): No (round 1: the commit-to-live metric measures only CI for single-commit cards; use a real start time), No (round 2: the FAIL condition rested on a test file), No (round 3: a WARN-only rewrite is a free exemption; require a named substitution verified line by line, and bound generated by a pattern list), and the last two conditions were adopted by the facilitator. Result: passed on the adopted wording; Codex has not re-voted on the final tightening of the rewrite and generated kinds (stricter, and consistent with its round 3 vote), which is recorded here for the retro.
- Open: the checker does not yet implement the limit, exception, slice-field, started_at, FLAGS.md and retro-order checks (card to file after card 195); docs/SLICING.md must publish the generated-file patterns; the architect role prompt in mob-defaults must change from confirm to decide SHIP AS IS or SPLIT; the gate-time work (filtered tests after red and green, slowest acceptance tests) is a board item.
- Reversal: all rules are documents and later checks; the numeric limits and the exceptions are reviewed at each retro.

<a id="d137"></a>
#### Decider D137: outside-in TDD under trunk-based main (the pending-test mechanism)

- Date: 2026-10-29. The product owner defined outside-in TDD (the text is in TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md, Practices): for one acceptance criterion, write a failing acceptance test first; identify the layer it consumes; build that layer with unit-level TDD using test doubles for its dependencies; then build the real implementation of each dependency from the layer above in the same way, again with doubles for its dependencies, until enough is built for the acceptance test to pass; the acceptance test fails during all of this until the last unit is built; then the next criterion. Addendum from the product owner: use ZOMBIES (Zero, One, Many, Boundaries, Interface, Exceptions) to find all of the acceptance criteria and each of the unit tests at each layer. The definition is not weakened. Question to the Decider: under trunk-based main with a green gate on every push, how does a committed but still failing acceptance test avoid turning main red.
- Seats by role (D109): architect, Codex: Yes (the mechanism below; alternatives rejected: a local-only red test leaves no auditable red commit, a skip attribute cannot prove failure, a separate excluded project can drift outside normal test execution). Independent auditor lens, Claude (Sonnet), fresh context: No on the first mechanism with five changes, all adopted. Result: passed on the adopted wording.
- Mechanism. (1) The red commit adds the acceptance test for ONE criterion with two traits: Status=Pending (already excluded from the gate by the filter Status!=Pending) and Criterion=NNN-K (card and criterion number). (2) Unit commits follow, layer by layer from the outside in, each green in the gate, each adding or changing a unit test file under the layer it builds (the file is named in the record) with test doubles for the dependencies. (3) The green commit removes Status=Pending from the acceptance test in the same commit that makes it pass; the acceptance test body is byte-identical from the red commit to the green commit apart from the traits. (4) A CI job pending-red runs the tests that carry Status=Pending and a Criterion trait and requires them to FAIL by assertion failure only (never by a build or discovery error); a pending test that passes fails the job (remove the marker). (5) The checker fails when more than one Criterion-tagged test is Pending at once, when a Criterion-tagged Pending test belongs to a closed card, when a Pending test has no record entry, or when a record criterion marked done still has Pending. (6) The trail record gets, per criterion, an entry criterion, acceptance_test, red_commit, unit_commits (in order, each with the unit test file it touches) and green_commit; the checker verifies against git log order (never the record alone) that the commits exist on main, the order red then units then green, that the red commit adds the named test with Pending and Criterion, that the green commit removes Pending from it, that the test body is unchanged, that each unit commit touches the named test file, and that criterion K+1 red comes after criterion K green. (7) ZOMBIES at both levels (added by the facilitator on the owner addendum; the seats were not re-polled, the checker card reviews it): the card acceptance criteria are derived with ZOMBIES, and the trail record lists, per card, the criteria with the ZOMBIES letters they cover, and per unit commit (per layer) the unit tests derived with ZOMBIES as zombies: one entry per letter Z, O, M, B, I, E, each either the name of a test that exists in the named unit test file at that commit or the text N/A because followed by a non-empty reason; the checker fails when a letter is missing, a named test does not exist in the file at that commit, or an N/A has no reason; every unit test uses doubles for the layer dependencies. (8) Judgment is FLAGGED, never failed: whether the ZOMBIES tests really cover the letter, an N/A reason that is not credible, the layer consumed and the dependency order, a unit test file with no test double, a trivial stub, whether the unit tests drive real design, production-side weakening in the green commit.
- Implications for card mode: the red seat writes ONE acceptance test for ONE criterion, not the whole card; the inner loop is unit red, green, refactor from the outside in with doubles; the acceptance criteria and the unit tests of every layer are derived with ZOMBIES; the acceptance test stays red until the last unit lands; then the next criterion starts; the records show the sequence per criterion and the ZOMBIES tests per layer (or N/A because per letter). A card with one criterion and many layers stays thin because the slice is three reviewable increments within the criterion (acceptance red; ordered layer-completion unit commits; acceptance green).
- Open: the checker, the pending-red job and the trail record fields are not implemented yet (a card after card 195); card mode prompts for the red and green seats must change to this sequence.
- Reversal: documents and later checks; removing the pending-red job turns the proof of real red off, which the external audit notices.

<a id="d138"></a>
#### Decider D138: review and signoff of Card 195 (the adapter and CLI projects are Avowry.*; first card run in card mode since 063)

- Date: 2026-10-29. Question: do the card's criteria hold on the card-mode result (commit 0aaabe4 and the seat records); is Card 195 Done. D137 stayed in force during the vote.
- Method: the final wording cut into nine clauses (items 1 to 8 of the mechanism and the card-mode implications); every seat voted on every clause, item 7 included. Task force and staffing as in D142 (architect, QA engineer, independent auditor, delivery lead, product manager, backend developer; each a fresh Claude Sonnet subagent context because Codex returned no response at its usage limit and Copilot, Grok and agy were out; the reason is recorded per seat).
- Round 1: no seat at No (all Support-it). Independent re-run of round 1: the backend developer at No on item 7 (six letters per unit commit is form over substance; the doubles sentence contradicted item 8), so the pass was not confirmed. Round 2 (item 7 amended, all clauses re-voted by fresh contexts): no seat at No (backend developer Yes, the others Support-it). Independent re-run of round 2 (six more fresh contexts): no seat at No; the outcome is confirmed. Every seat's per-clause vote is committed in .mob/records/deciders/D143-votes.json.

D137 stands on its final wording except item 7, which is replaced by: (7) ZOMBIES at both levels (not votable: the product owner's direction is that ZOMBIES finds the acceptance criteria AND each layer's unit tests; vote on the record form). The card acceptance criteria are derived with ZOMBIES, and the trail record lists, per card, the criteria with the ZOMBIES letters they cover. Per layer (not per commit), at the commit that completes that layer, the record lists the unit tests derived with ZOMBIES as zombies: one entry per letter Z, O, M, B, I, E, each either the exact name of a test method that exists and is executable (not skipped, not empty) in the named unit test file at that commit, or N/A because with a non-empty reason; one reason may cover several letters. The checker fails only on mechanical facts: a missing letter, a named test method absent, skipped or empty at that commit, or an N/A with no reason; it prints every N/A reason in its output for the audit. Whether a test really covers its letter, and whether the unit tests use doubles for the layer dependencies (leaf layers excepted), are judgment and FLAGGED under item 8, never failed.
- Support-it requests carried to the checker cards (not part of the rule): define assertion failure by the runner's result type (a NotImplementedException or a build, discovery or setup error is not a valid red) and give the pending-red job its own red fixtures; define the test-body comparison (method text with attribute lines removed); define skipped and empty (a Skip argument; a body with no statements); the record names the unit commit that completes each layer; every unit commit adds or changes a test method; the one-Pending limit is per card; leaf layers are excepted in item 2 as in item 7; a scaffold fills the record fields; revisit the per-layer cost at the first retro after real use.
- Reversal: a later Decider; the rules are documents until the checker and the pending-red job land.

- Date: 2026-10-30. Replaces the failed D139, D140, D141 and D144. The product owner sent the D144 conflict (how the probe log is verified) back to the team as a reversible implementation detail, not a direction question, and asked for the smallest reversible step, a fresh Decider with all seats and an independent re-run, and the voting rule that a No names one fixed condition which is adopted and re-polled when reasonable and reversible.
- Seats: architect, QA engineer, independent auditor, delivery lead, product manager, backend developer (added on the domain-expert question: the seat that builds the checks), each a fresh Claude Sonnet context per round; reason per seat: probes at 04:03 UTC, under 60 minutes old at seat time, show Codex, Copilot, Grok and agy without usable quota (.mob/records/probes/2026-10-30.jsonl).
- Rounds: round 1, QA engineer and backend developer at No on C2 (corrective cards could not land while F-001 blocks), condition adopted; round 2, backend developer at No on C2 and C7 (the mob-defaults facilitator's exit 5 refused every card start, so corrective cards could never start), condition adopted; round 3, architect and delivery lead at No on C2 (a corrective card commit must also carry its own record, the regenerated board archive and appended probe lines), condition adopted; round 4, no seat at No (four Yes, two Support-it). Independent re-run by six fresh contexts on the round 4 text: no seat at No; the outcome is confirmed. Every seat's per-clause vote and condition is in .mob/records/deciders/D145-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. The adopted text follows (not votable parts as stated in the proposal: the four product owner ship refusals and probe age, open findings stop the line, D135).
- C1 Findings file. .mob/findings.json, the file the mob-defaults facilitator already reads (add !.mob/findings.json to .gitignore). Base schema is mob-defaults: id, owner team, practice, corrective_action, status open or closed, closure (auditor, closed_by, evidence commit), audits_open, escalated, guardrail. Avowry adds: kind FAIL or FLAG, opened, summary, corrective_paths (explicit files, no wildcards) or closing_evidence (the file a closing commit adds or changes), closing_commits (append-only). Frozen once committed: id, kind, opened, practice, summary, corrective_action, corrective_paths, closing_evidence. A finding blocks until closed with an auditor who is not closed_by and an evidence commit that is an ancestor of main; closed is terminal. The commit creating the file may list pre-existing closing commits for any entry whose evidence predates it (bootstrap true). No entry or the file may be deleted. Main is linear (check rejects merge commits in scope).

C2 Stop the line, from the commit creating the file; the creating commit itself is exempt from the lanes and is checked against the auditor's register (C3). A finding blocks while it is not validly closed (C1), FAIL or FLAG alike (the mob-defaults definition). While any FAIL blocks, each commit is (a) a process commit: no Card subject, the subject has an exact F-NNN token of a blocking finding or starts with Retro or Decider, and it changes only MOB.md, DRIVERS.md, HANDOFF.md, docs/decisions/, docs/retros/, .mob/records/deciders/, .mob/records/reviews/, .mob/records/probes/, the status, closure and closing_commits fields of .mob/findings.json, or board/ as the regenerated archive; or (b) a corrective commit: its subject has the exact token of a blocking finding, FAIL or FLAG (a Card subject only for a card whose GitHub issue names that finding, read through gh as the existing timeline checks do, failing closed when unreadable) and every changed file is in that finding's corrective_paths, .mob/findings.json, MOB.md, the card's own .mob/records/NNN.json (NNN the card number in the subject), board/ as the regenerated archive, or new lines appended to .mob/records/probes/*.jsonl. At all times, a change to TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md, scripts/mob_process.py, scripts/check-*.py, .githooks/ or .github/workflows/ needs in its subject a D-number whose record is in a strict ancestor commit, in force or applied, with a committed votes file showing no seat at No in its final round and its independent re-run. Authority: the previous-version checker run and the external audit's pinned copy, never the new head's checker alone; every head descends from the last audited head.

C3 Initial content: the file mirrors every finding in the external audit's register; the auditor checks the creating commit against its register. Known to the team: F-001 (66cec11, awaiting closure); F-002, F-003, F-006 (closing_evidence D142, D143 and their votes files, bootstrap); F-004 (.mob/records/reviews/195-final.md, 75d775c, bootstrap); F-005 (.mob/records/reviews/195-takeover.md, to be written by a non-author seat); F-007, F-028 to F-030, F-032 to F-035 (closed by the auditor at audit 15, bootstrap); F-008 FLAG no detector (corrective_paths scripts/mob_process.py, tests/test_mob_process.py, tests/test_mob_process_findings.py, tests/test_mob_process_staffing.py, scripts/probe_drivers.py, tests/test_probe_drivers.py, docs/schemas/trail-record.schema.json, docs/AUDIT-CHECKLIST.md, .gitignore); F-037, F-038 (427c8b7 and the votes files, bootstrap); F-040 (53946f6 and .mob/records/probes/, bootstrap).

C4 Record fields for cards whose first Card commit descends from the commit recording D145; the mob-defaults facilitator writes .mob/records/NNN.json and these fields extend it: started_at (later of the first Card commit date and its first CI run created time, both committed), task_force (role, domain_or_context, model, context_id, why), left_out, seats (role, model, context_id, quota_reason), probe line references, domain_expert per boundary (refinement plus one per acceptance criterion), additions (with a passed Decider id), retro_decisions.

C5 Ship rules, hard, red first, over the whole C4 window: the four product owner conditions; undeclared task force; missing domain_expert answer; addition without a passed Decider; a member in any record version from the first Card commit on and absent later without a retro_decisions remove entry.

C6 SPOTCHECK (the smallest step). Probe evidence in .mob/records/probes/*.jsonl, append-only, one line per probe, written by the committed probe script: driver, probed_at, command (Codex pinned to the seat model), raw at most 2 KB, usable_quota a strict boolean computed by that script. check rejects an edit to an earlier line, a non-boolean usable_quota, raw over 2 KB, a probed_at later than its commit, and a Claude seat without a probe line under 60 minutes old for every non-Claude model. The external auditor spot-checks at least one probe line per audit against the live CLI and records the result. EXPERIMENT E7 (Sandbox), recorded but not built: if a spot-check finds a mismatch between a logged line and reality, the team builds the isolated recompute of usable_quota (audit hook, no network, writes, spawn, exec or ctypes, 10 second timeout); metric: spot-check mismatches per audit; reversal: drop E7 after three clean audits.

C7 Delivery, thin cards within D136 limits (a card that would exceed them is split, never waived), red first, each citing F-008 so it is a corrective commit under C2 (b): A1 findings file with the C1 and C3 checks and docs/AUDIT-CHECKLIST.md; A2 the C2 lanes and the MOB.md listing; D the four product owner conditions with the C6 log checks; C the C4 fields with the facilitator's record; E the task-force rules. Order A1, A2, D, C, E. Stop the line means the next card is the fix (TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md, CI on main): the mob-defaults facilitator's exit 5 admits only a card whose GitHub issue names a blocking finding as its corrective action (the same issue-names-finding test as C2 b, failing closed when the issue is unreadable); that facilitator change is part of card A1, red test first, and recorded in mob-defaults with a reference to D145. Not votable (product owner rule): no other card starts while any finding blocks, FLAG included; so product cards resume once F-008 and every other finding is validly closed, after E.
- Reversal: a later Decider; E7 is recorded, not built, and is dropped after three clean audits. The detectors land as cards A1, A2, D, C, E, each citing F-008.

<a id="d150"></a>
#### Decider D150: ceremonies besides the retro (two kept as experiments, three dropped)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Trigger: the product owner's hypothesis that ceremonies besides the retro may help, with Dispatch's five starting proposals; each kept ceremony states its user benefit, metric and counter-metric, its own accountability check, trigger and cost; ceremonies that do not earn their cost are dropped. Proposer: agile coach seat. Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI (one throwaway clone each): round 1 architect, QA engineer, independent reviewer (audit lens), delivery lead, product manager, all Support-it; independent re-run by five fresh seats (product manager replaced by agile coach), all Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D150-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Every kept ceremony is an experiment over the next 5 cards, then keep, revise or drop at that retro.
- Adopted text:
  C1 Three Amigos refinement before the red seat: KEEP AS EXPERIMENT.
  - User benefit: the persona gets criteria a user can observe, derived with ZOMBIES, with the domain-expert question answered before code, so fewer rework cards.
  - Metric: share of cards whose review-fix commits (Card NNN review fixes) are zero. Counter-metric: minutes from card pull to red commit; also criteria count per card (watch for over-specification, ruling 6).
  - Accountability check: git shows a commit adding the criteria and the domain-expert answer to the card record, authored by PM, QA and dev seats in separate contexts, that precedes the first red commit for that card.
  - Trigger: every card pull, after the re-rank. Cost: about 3 short parallel seat calls, capped at one round; skip the domain-expert call when the architect slice answer is no.
  C2 Review or demo after Done: KEEP AS EXPERIMENT.
  - User benefit: the product owner sees working behaviour, not claims; catches a Done card that passes tests but fails the persona.
  - Metric: share of Done cards whose retro holds a persona-run observation. Counter-metric: seat minutes per card spent on demos, and demos that only repeat the test output.
  - Accountability check: the card retro file in docs/retros/ contains the command and its real output run as the persona, committed before the issue closes; at each milestone or every 5 cards a demo summary file is committed and linked from the retro.
  - Trigger: each Done; summary every 5th card or milestone. Cost: about 2 seat minutes per card, about 10 minutes per summary, run by a seat that did not author the card.
  C3 Planning or re-rank at each loop boundary: DROP as a new ceremony.
  - Reason: already in force by D148 with its own committed note in .mob/records/reranks/; adding it here would duplicate it. D148 keeps its own accountability check.
  C4 Periodic backlog refinement: DROP.
  - Reason: grooming cards below the top of Ready is cards written far ahead of their turn, which ruling 6 names as the over-specification to avoid; cards decay as evidence arrives. Just-in-time refinement is covered by C1 and the D148 re-rank of the top of Ready. Reopen if a pulled card repeatedly fails the Ready check.
  C5 Standup or sync at cycle boundaries: DROP.
  - Reason: WIP is one card (concurrent class under Bishop's Law), so there is nothing to resync; the loop boundary re-rank and the retro already sync. Reopen when two or more parallel-class worktrees run at once, with the resync cost measured then.

<a id="d151"></a>
#### Decider D151: the stand-down, a micro-retro at each cycle boundary within a card (amends D150)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Trigger: the product owner prefers a stand-down (a micro-retro) to a standup, replaced by the full retro at Done. Proposer: facilitator, on the product owner's input. Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI: round 1 architect, QA engineer, independent reviewer (audit lens), delivery lead, agile coach, all Support-it; independent re-run (product manager in place of the agile coach), all Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D151-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  S1 Standup stays dropped (D150 C5; the product owner prefers a stand-down).
  S2 Stand-down at each cycle boundary within a card: KEEP AS EXPERIMENT (next 5 cards, then keep, revise or drop at that retro, like the other D150 experiments).
  - What: at each cycle boundary within a card (after a green or refactor turn, before the next criterion or cycle starts), a few lines: what worked, what did not, ONE adjustment applied in the next cycle. Written by a seat of the card's task force in its own context (not the facilitator's), rotating among the members.
  - Where: the card record .mob/records/NNN.json, a stand_downs list with one entry per cycle boundary (cycle, worked, did_not, adjustment, seat context), committed with that boundary's commit. It sits next to the domain-expert answer the same boundary already records.
  - At Done: the full per-card retro takes its place; no stand-down is written at the last boundary, and the retro reads the stand-downs as input.
  - User benefit: problems found in one cycle are fixed in the next one of the same card instead of waiting for the retro, so fewer review-fix commits and shorter cards.
  - Metric: share of stand-down adjustments that the next cycle visibly applies (the next commit or seat record shows the change). Counter-metric: seat minutes per stand-down (target a couple of minutes, a few lines), and stand-downs that repeat the same text or say nothing.
  - Accountability check: for a card with N cycle boundaries before Done, the record holds N-1 stand-downs, each with a non-empty adjustment and the context of a task-force seat, committed in or before the boundary's commit; none after Done; the retro file names them as input.
  S3 The rest of D150 stands: Three Amigos refinement and the persona demo stay experiments; planning (covered by the D148 re-rank) and periodic backlog refinement stay dropped.

<a id="d153"></a>
#### Decider D153: a lighter path for routine per-card decisions (objection windows; full Deciders for objections, one-way doors and rule changes)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Trigger: Dispatch observed (sent with the product owner's approval) that card 205 generated about seven full Deciders (D146 to D152), mostly routine per-card calls, before any code landed. A standing-rule change (it amends the Decider protocol, the D148 re-rank and the F-038 re-run guardrail for routine calls), so it took the full Decider. Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI. Round 1: three Support-it; the QA engineer and the audit-lens reviewer No with one condition each, both adopted. Round 2: all Support-it. Independent re-run: all Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D153-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  L1 Routine per-card decisions take an objection window instead of a full Decider: the task-force declaration, the slice decision, the loop-boundary re-rank (amends D148, which says decided by Decider), card-text and declaration fixes, and a mid-card addition. The product manager seat proposes in one committed note; every declared role seat of the card, each in its own fresh context, records either no objection or an objection with ONE fixed condition.
  L2 A full Decider with an independent re-run happens only when a seat objects (and its condition is not simply adopted and re-polled in the window), when the decision is a one-way door (hard to reverse: a publish, a deletion, a schema or data migration, a public claim), or when it changes a standing rule (the agreement, a D-record in force, the checker). Long-term rule changes keep the full Decider.
  L3 Accountability: each objection window is committed (.mob/records/decisions/NNN-<slug>.json: proposal, each seat with role, model, context and no objection or the condition, start time, probe reference); removals stay retro-only; the F-038 independent re-run applies to every full Decider and not to an objection window with no objection; the external auditor spot-checks windows and may FAIL one that should have been a full Decider. The auditor's Decider checks need updating to match; the team tells the auditor in the same commit.
  L3a (audit-lens condition, adopted): each window is a trail of separate commits in this order: the product manager proposal note, which states why the decision is neither a one-way door nor a standing-rule change; then each seat reply as its own commit with its probe reference; then the decision commit; an adopted condition and its re-poll appear as further commits in the same trail.
  L3b (QA engineer condition, adopted in the nearest form the standing rules allow): a checker rule, written red first with a failing fixture committed before the rule, fails any .mob/records/decisions/NNN-<slug>.json window that lacks a category from the closed L1 list, a window end time, or, for any declared role seat, its model, context, no-objection-or-condition entry and probe reference. It cannot land in the commit that records D153 (D145 C2: a checker change needs a D-number whose record is in a strict ancestor commit), so it lands as the next card after 205, red first, and no window counts toward the L4 experiment until it is on main; until then the external auditor checks windows by hand against the same fields.
  L4 Experiment for the next 5 cards. Metric: decisions per card and minutes from card declared to first red commit. Counter-metric: routine decisions later reversed or found wrong, and windows the auditor rules should have been full Deciders. The retro after the fifth card keeps, revises or drops it.

<a id="d154"></a>
#### Decider D154: the card 205 retro process pick (the claim-equals-commit and pre-ship evidence gate, a 5-card experiment)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 205 retro (candidates: A, the claim-equals-commit and pre-ship evidence gate; B, an overlap check at slice). Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI. Round 1: architect, QA engineer, product manager Yes; audit-lens reviewer and delivery lead Support-it. Independent re-run (agile coach in place of the product manager): four Yes, one Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D154-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D154, the card 205 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: candidate A only (claim-equals-commit and pre-ship evidence gate). B is not adopted now.
  Why A: four of seven voices proposed it, and it answers the costliest card 205 failure (a closure-blocking defect found after sign-off, audit 22). Why not both: B adds a table at slice, which leans toward up-front specification (VISION ruling 6) and overlaps the Three Amigos criteria (D150, still on trial). The architect stand-down already made listing overlaps at slice a rule applied in Done check 3. Running two new gates on the same cards would also blur which one moved the metric. Reopen B at the trial retro if undeclared born-green criteria recur.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that requiring a pushed commit hash for every done, PASS or evidence claim, plus a pre-ship dry run of the closure record check and a resolve-on-origin check of every cited commit and record path, will cut evidence-integrity defects for the whole mob. We will know when claimed-before-committed or record-check findings fall from the card 205 baseline of at least 2 to 0 per card, and closure-blocking defects after sign-off fall from 1 to 0.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  1. No seat writes done, PASS or evidence unless it cites a pushed commit hash that contains it.
  2. Before ship, the delivery lead runs the closure record check in dry-run mode and confirms every commit hash and record path in the closure record resolves on origin (clean clone or worktree).
  3. Output of both checks is committed in the card record before the issue closes.
  METRIC: per card, count of claimed-before-committed or record-check findings, and closure-blocking defects after sign-off (target 0, judged not stamped).
  COUNTER-METRICS: minutes from sign-off to close; extra commits per card; checks that only repeat green output without resolving a path (judged by the auditor).
  Metrics are evidence, not targets. During the bootstrap phase no drop on metrics alone; keep, revise or drop is judgment through a Decider.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 5 cards, starting with 213, matching the D150 experiments. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK (what in git proves it ran): for each of the 5 cards, a commit under .mob/records/ holding the dry-run output and the origin-resolution list, timestamped before the closing commit, and every done or PASS line in the card record names a hash that git cat-file confirms on origin. A card without that commit counts as not run, and the auditor tick may FAIL it.
  BASELINE (card 205): at least 2 claimed-before-committed or record-check findings (evidence claimed before it was in git; record check comparing seats by model); 1 closure-blocking defect after sign-off (fixed in f04ac7f, bd17f7a and a third commit). For B (recorded, not trialed): 2 undeclared born-green criteria of 3.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Revert by deleting the rule text and dropping the check step; no shared state changes.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d155"></a>
#### Decider D155: product owner ruling (not a team vote): every stand-down and retro opens by reviewing each previous adjustment

- Date: 2026-10-30. Recorded as a decision record so that rule-text changes can cite it (D145 C2). Not a team vote: product owner direction, given with audit 24.
- Ruling: every stand-down and every retro opens by reviewing each adjustment the team made at the previous stand-down or retro, before any new adjustment. For each adjustment the review states: (1) the issue it targeted; (2) whether it resolved that issue, with evidence (a metric or a commit before and after); (3) its side effects, good and bad; (4) a verdict of keep, tune or revert. The review is recorded in the card record (the in-progress record during the card, then the retro).
- First application: card 213's next stand-down reviews stand-down 1's adjustment (shared fixture helper; module-only test runs until the pre-ship gate): did module-only runs shorten feedback, and did deferring the full suite let anything slip to pre-ship?
- The rule goes into TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md, and a mob-defaults card brings it to the template and to the loop runner's record fields (loop.py).

  C1. Declare the 214 task force as in the proposal field of .mob/records/decisions/214-taskforce.json (its first part): PM ready ctx-1, architect ctx-6, QA red ctx-2, backend developer [author] ctx-3, reviewer with test-infrastructure and Kestrel lens ctx-4, delivery lead ctx-7, PM signoff ctx-5. No separate .NET/Kestrel expert (developer and reviewer cover it); a hosting or CI expert may join later through a Decider.
