<a id="d157"></a>
#### Decider D157: the card 214 retro process pick (one acceptance criterion per red-green cycle, checked from git; a 5-card experiment)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 214 retro (docs/retros/card-214-2026-10-30.md; seven candidates). It adds enforcement only: the rule "one acceptance criterion at a time" is the product owner's (audit 28, F-060). Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI, model sonnet: round 1 product manager, architect, QA engineer, backend developer, independent reviewer (audit lens), delivery lead (three Yes, three Support-it); independent re-run by six fresh seats with the agile coach in place of the product manager (four Yes, two Support-it). Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D157-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D157, the card 214 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: candidate C only (enforce one acceptance criterion per red-green cycle, checked from git). The other six are deferred, not rejected.
  Why C: three of seven voices (PM, developer, delivery lead) proposed it, and it answers the costliest card 214 miss: about 800 lines and ten guards of red before any green, one green holding three criteria, first push 11:06. The product owner already ruled the rule (audit 28, F-060), so C re-decides nothing. It only makes the rule checkable, since the audit lens found "nothing in git enforces it".
  Why not the others now: (a) window cap per card changes D153, whose 5-card trial is still running; cap it at the D153 trial retro, where six windows on 214 is evidence. (b) known-red manifest, committed gate script and rebase-map check all tune D154, at trial card 2 of 5. Tuning it mid-trial blurs what D154 moved; take them to the D154 trial retro as one tune bundle. (c) The stale-probe check is a tooling fix (F-040). Running C beside D150, D153 and D154 is the last new rule until a trial ends.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that requiring each red commit to name exactly one slice criterion, and the next commit to be that criterion's green before another red, will cut held greens and issue-to-first-green. We will know when criteria per green commit falls from 3 to 1 and issue to first pushed green falls from 179 minutes toward under 90.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. The slice record numbers the card's acceptance criteria. Each red commit body carries one line "Criterion: n" naming exactly one.
  C2. A red commit for criterion n is followed by its green before any red for criterion n+1 (the first red may be committed in advance only if the slice states why).
  C3. The green is pushed at once (D154 claim-equals-commit); a green held locally needs an in-flight note.
  C4. The D154 pre-ship gate output adds one line: the git log order of Criterion lines, with any breach explained. This is a line in the existing gate, not a new gate.
  METRIC: per card, criteria per green commit (target 1) and issue to first pushed green.
  COUNTER-METRICS: issue to first red (214: 83 minutes; 205: 24); commits per card and lines per commit within D136; review findings per card. Evidence, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 5 cards, the same window as D150 and D154. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK (what in git proves it ran): for each card, git log shows a Criterion: line in every red commit body, red and green alternating by criterion, and the gate output committed under .mob/records/ before the closing commit lists that order. A card without it counts as not run and the auditor tick may FAIL it.
  BASELINE (card 214): 3 criteria red (about 800 lines, ten guards, 26681e1 to 391828f, 09:30 to 09:47) before one green; local green about 10:35, pushed 11:06 (111960d, afa4e40); issue 08:07 to first pushed green 179 minutes; issue to first red 83 minutes.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Revert by deleting the rule text and the gate line; no shared state or earlier D-record changes.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d158"></a>
#### Decider D158: the card 209 retro process pick (a facilitator pre-window checklist; a 5-card experiment)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 209 retro (docs/retros/card-209-2026-10-30.md; seven candidates). Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI, model sonnet: round 1 product manager, architect, QA engineer, backend developer, independent reviewer (audit lens), delivery lead (five Yes, one Support-it); independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager (four Yes, two Support-it). Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D158-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D158, the card 209 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: candidate G only (a facilitator pre-window checklist: seats, caps, working directory, line endings). The other six are deferred, not rejected.
  Why G: it is the one lever wholly outside D157. Card 209 had four facilitator misses (slice and ship seats left out, a cap from an estimate, a verification run in the wrong repository, wrong line endings told to a developer). Each was caught late; the wrong-repository run cost about 17 minutes. G changes no checker and no earlier rule.
  Why not the others now: (a) The Criterion or "Review fix: Fn" line, the trailer lint and the line-ending check all tune D157, at trial card 1 of 5. Changing C1 now would blur what D157 moved, and card 209 is its only evidence. Take the fix-commit gap (73026aa, 7fdb168, e239b12 carry no Criterion line) to the D157 trial retro as one tune bundle; it is recorded there as evidence now. (b) The PM proposal-seat checklist is covered by C1 below. (c) Affected-tests-first changes the D154 gate; card 217 (stale gates) comes first.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe a short checklist, committed before each task-force window, will remove repeat facilitator misses. We will know when misses per card fall from 4 to 1 or fewer.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. The proposal lists every D154 required seat, including slice and ship, each present or removed with a reason.
  C2. Every in-flight cap names its source: the seat's recorded start time, or the measured suite time (about 17 minutes) plus margin. An estimate may not be the only source.
  C3. Before a verification run, the facilitator prints the working directory and git rev-parse --show-toplevel in the same command; the run record shows it.
  C4. A line-ending instruction to a seat comes from git ls-files --eol, not memory.
  C5. The checklist is one block under .mob/records/ for the card, committed before the window commit.
  METRIC: facilitator misses per card from the retro-input list (target 1 or fewer).
  COUNTER-METRICS: issue to first red (209: 41 minutes; 214: 83; 205: 24); delay from pull to window opening; ceremony lines per card. Evidence, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 5 cards, the same window as D150, D153, D154 and D157. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per card, git shows the checklist block committed before the window commit, with the seats, cap sources and printed repository. A card without it counts as not run and the auditor tick may FAIL it.
  BASELINE (card 209): 4 facilitator misses; issue to first red 41 minutes; pull to sign-off 3 h 41 min; 5 full-suite runs of about 17 minutes, one in the wrong repository.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Revert by deleting the checklist rule; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d161"></a>
#### Decider D161: product owner direction (not a team vote): lock the mob process down before product work; reports describe, not cite

- Date: 2026-10-30. Recorded as a decision record so that rule-text changes can cite it (D145 C2). Not a team vote: product owner direction.
- Direction 1 (the goal behind the current order), in the product owner's words: "the mob process needs fixing because it keeps drifting. once we can lock it down and save it to the default template then we can build anything safely." Process-first is intentional: the goal is a locked-down process, enforced by tooling and saved into the mob-defaults template, after which product work resumes. Every re-rank uses this goal: work that gets the rules enforced in the mob-defaults template and the loop runner is the priority.
- Direction 2 (reporting): in anything meant for the product owner or the person running the mob, cards, decisions and findings are described by what they are or do; a bare card number, D-number or finding ID is never used alone; an ID may follow in parentheses.

<a id="d163"></a>
#### Decider D163: product owner clarification (not a team vote): balanced model rotation, not a rule against Claude

- Date: 2026-10-30. Not a team vote: product owner direction on the check that just landed in card 208 criterion 2 (a Claude seat starts only while no other model has quota). The product owner's words: "I'm not sure I want a 'no Claude' rule per se. I just want the rotation to even out and don't just jump to all Claude like has happened in the past."
- Intent: balanced rotation. Seat the least-recently-used available model first, so load spreads across Codex, Copilot, Grok, agy and Claude (all finite prepaid allotments). The failure mode to stop is defaulting to all-Claude while others have capacity. Claude taking its share of seats is fine. No new strictness beyond this intent.
- The team decides by Decider how to rework the just-landed check (mechanism and metric); one candidate offered, not mandated: pick the least-used available model per seat, and refuse an all-Claude roster, or a Claude share above some ratio, while others are available. This is a correction to a just-finished card and is handled as one.

<a id="d164"></a>
#### Decider D164: correct card 208's quota check to least-recently-used seating (balanced rotation, D163)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Trigger: the product owner's clarification D163 (balanced rotation, not a rule against Claude) on the just-landed card 208 criterion 2, and the card 208 review (c2ecd10). Proposer: architect seat. Round 1: five Yes or Support-it; the independent reviewer (audit lens) voted No in round 1 and in its re-run with one condition (the seat-use history must be a tracked, committed, append-only file, so rotation can be recomputed from git), adopted under D145. Round 2: four Yes and two Support-it; independent re-run by six fresh seats: six Yes. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D164-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED as amended. Adopted text:
  Decider D164, correct card 208 criterion 2 to balanced rotation (per D163)
  PICK: replace "no Claude seat while another model has quota" with least-recently-used seating.
  C1. Mechanism and history: each seat goes to the available model (enabled, probe ok) whose last seating is oldest. The history is derived, not counted: the facilitator copies each seat's events.jsonl line (model, role, card, time, probe taken_at) into the tracked append-only file .mob/records/seat-use.jsonl at gate_seat, after the backend call is chosen; a seat has no write path to it. The file is committed with each seating commit or the card commit, never rewritten, never deleted. The gate computes LRU from it; an auditor recomputes LRU and the all-Claude count from git alone. Claude is refused only when another available model is older; the refusal names it. LRU over a ratio cap: no invented number, and an all-Claude roster is impossible while others have quota.
  C2. Survives from criterion 2: 60-minute probe freshness and re-probe; an unread probe result is never read as quota; SeatRefused naming role, context and model; the quota_reason field built from the probe in force.
  C3. Reverted: Claude starts only once every other model is exhausted. Claude taking its LRU turn, or any turn when others lack quota, is allowed. Nothing stricter than D163 is added.
  C4. Metric: seat share per model per card and per day, and rosters all-Claude while another model had quota (target 0), both from seat-use.jsonl. Counter-metric: refusals and re-probes per card, turns delayed by refusals.
  C5. Delivery: a correction to card 208 before it closes, not a new card; its landed commits (6af1cca, 56b3686, 832ca6a, 36f09a0) are the work corrected. Criterion 2 is rewritten, red first, then green, one criterion per cycle (D157). The 208 review (c2ecd10) findings are folded in as tests: F1 the unusable-pin branch; F2 TrueMobbing asserts the joiner's own vote; F3 a test that the seat's Claude model equals the probe's model (today tied only by key order). Which Claude model family seats use is left to the product owner.
  C6. REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Revert by restoring the quota_first_problem gate and removing the history read; seat-use.jsonl is kept, not deleted. D163 stands.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d165"></a>
#### Decider D165: the card 208 retro pick (an intent line before the first red; the feedback-loop lens as a standing retro section)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 208 retro (docs/retros/card-208-2026-10-30.md), where all seven voices proposed the same candidate after the quota rule was built to the letter of the finding instead of the product owner's intent (corrected by D164). The feedback-loop lens is the product owner and Dispatch's proposal (.mob/records/208/retro-proposal-nested-loops.md). Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI, model sonnet: round 1 three Yes and three Support-it; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: two Yes and four Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D165-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D165, the card 208 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: an intent line before the first red (the candidate all seven voices shared), confirmed by the facilitator against recorded product owner direction. Other levers are deferred, not rejected.
  Why: card 208's criterion 2 was built to the letter of a finding, not the owner's intent. The intent arrived at review and sign-off (D163, D164) and cost a correction cycle. Check intent where it is cheapest, before the red.
  Why not the others now: shuffled-order runs, a navigator seat and runner-stamped probe age are separate levers; one experiment only.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe a one-line intent restatement confirmed before the first red removes intent-miss rework. We will know when intent-caused corrections after review fall from 1 per card to 0.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Before the first red of a criterion that implements a finding, MD item or owner direction, the red seat writes "Intent: <what the owner wants and why>" in its record, quoting the owner and naming the D-number or finding.
  C2. The facilitator confirms it against recorded direction (D-records, issue text, blockers). A match is "confirmed"; the product owner is not needed.
  C3. If it cannot be traced to a recorded statement it is "unconfirmed": the facilitator asks the owner one purpose question (what is wanted, not how). The red waits at most 30 minutes, then proceeds marked unconfirmed, and review checks that line first.
  C4. One sentence in the existing red record; no new file, seat or meeting. Mechanical criteria are marked "no intent question".
  METRIC: intent-caused corrections after review, per card, from git (target 0).
  COUNTER-METRICS: slice to first red (208: 39 minutes); owner questions per card; unconfirmed lines per card; red-to-green minutes. Evidence, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 cards with behavioural criteria, the window of D150 to D158. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per card, git shows each red's Intent line with status and source. A criterion without it counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it.
  BASELINE (card 208): 1 correction cycle after sign-off-time clarification (69 minutes red to green, about 3 h); 6 full runs of about 20 minutes; window to sign-off about 6 h.
  C5. LENS: the team adopts the feedback-loop lens (each loop mapped by who gives feedback, latency, what the team does with it) as a standing retro section; a card puts it into the mob-defaults template (D161). A lens, not a second experiment.
  C6. Missing loops, candidates for later picks, not this one: pairing (no live steering; Dispatch's navigator), feature (no persona demo), epic (no LLM-customer benchmark). Slow: lifecycle (owner input at sign-off), slice (pollution found by the 20-minute run).
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Delete the intent rule and the template section; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d166"></a>
#### Decider D166: the card 219 retro pick (An assumption line with a probe in the slice of any card that changes a rule, the ranking or the checker)

- Date: 2026-10-30. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 219 retro (docs/retros/card-219-2026-10-30.md). Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI, model sonnet: round 1 3 Yes, 3 Support-it; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 3 Yes, 3 Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D166-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D166, the card 219 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: an assumption line in the slice for any card that changes a rule, the ranking or the checker (five of seven voices; the MOB.md freshness check is deferred, not rejected).
  Why: the record checker assumed cards close in number order; re-ranking (D160) made that false and nobody looked, so signed-off cards 209 and 208 were held for several cards. Check the invariant where the change is made, not where a card trips on it.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe naming the invariants a change touches, with one real-history probe each, removes stuck cards caused by a stale invariant. We will know when cards held by such an assumption fall from 2 to 0.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. A card whose slice changes a rule, the ranking or order of cards, or the checker adds one line to the slice: "Assumes: <invariant the checker or gate relies on>; probe: <a real record or the current ranking it was tried against>". One line per invariant, at most three.
  C2. The architect writes it; the reviewer checks the probe at review. A line without a probe is "unprobed" and review checks it first.
  C3. Cards that change none of these write "no invariant change". No new file, seat or meeting.
  C4. This is a slice line only. It is not an intent line (D165, red record), not a facilitator checklist item (D158), and not a red-green rule (D157).
  METRIC: cards held or blocked by an invariant that stopped being true, per card, from git (target 0).
  COUNTER-METRICS: slice minutes added; unprobed lines per card; lines that name no real invariant (ceremony). Evidence, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 cards that match C1, the window of D150 to D158 and D165. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider. Cards outside C1 do not count.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per card, git shows the slice line or "no invariant change". A matching card without it counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it.
  NO BLUR: D157, D158 and D165 keep their own metrics and cards. Retros score each on its own record: D157 from cycles, D158 from the facilitator checklist, D165 from Intent lines, D166 from slice assumption lines. A D166 catch is not credited to the others, and a card's outcome is tagged to the rule that caught it.
  BASELINE (card 219): 2 signed-off cards (209, 208) held by an ordering assumption; both released only by a one-card waiver (D162) and card 219 (about 55 minutes to green).
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Delete the slice line; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d167"></a>
#### Decider D167: the card 226 retro pick (A hostile-input list in the slice of any criterion that takes a list or map of names, answered entry by entry in the red)

- Date: 2026-10-31. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 226 retro (docs/retros/card-226-2026-10-31.md). Seats through decider.py with the headless claude CLI, model sonnet: round 1 3 Yes, 3 Support-it; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 3 Yes, 3 Support-it. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D167-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D167, the card 226 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: a hostile-input list for any criterion that takes a list or map of names: the architect writes it in the slice, the red seat answers each entry in the red commit body, the reviewer checks it first (six of seven voices; the reviewer's commit-message claim check is deferred, not rejected).
  Why: card 226's three reds asserted what the slice named, not what the runner does with other inputs. Review then found, after three clean greens, seat-use lines written for disabled, unknown and repeated seats (F1) and a voter model tied to the probe only by the order of a models map (F2). Name the awkward inputs where the criterion is written and answer them where the test is written, not at review.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe a four-entry hostile-input list in the slice, answered one by one in the red, catches input-space gaps before review. We will know when review findings of the input-gap kind fall from 2 to 0 per card.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. A criterion that takes a list, set or map of names (seats, backends, models, rankings) gets one slice line: "Inputs: disabled: <case>; unknown: <case>; repeated: <case>; reordered: <case>". Each of the four entries names a case or says "n/a: <reason>".
  C2. The architect writes the slice line. The red seat answers it in the red commit body: one line per entry, "disabled: <test name>" or "n/a: <reason>", and the same for the other three. A test name is a test that exists in that red.
  C3. The reviewer checks the red body against the slice line first. An entry with no test name and no "n/a: reason" is "not run". An "n/a" with no reason counts as not run.
  C4. Criteria that take no list or map write "no list input". No new file, seat or meeting.
  C5. This is not D166's assumption line (the invariant a checker or gate relies on, a slice line on cards that change a rule, the ranking or the checker). It is not D165's intent line (what the owner wants, in the red record). It is not a facilitator checklist item (D158) and not a red-green rule (D157). One list per criterion, in the slice, answered in the red; it is not repeated in the green.
  METRIC: review findings per card that are input-space gaps (a disabled, unknown, repeated or reordered input the criterion's tests never fed), from the review record (target 0).
  COUNTER-METRICS: slice minutes added; red-to-green minutes per criterion (226: 37, 48, 40); tests added per criterion; "n/a" entries per card with a thin reason (ceremony). Evidence, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 cards with a criterion that takes a list or map of names, the window of D150 to D158, D165 and D166. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider. Cards outside C1 do not count.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per card, git shows the slice Inputs line (or "no list input") and, in each red commit body for that criterion, an answer for every entry. A matching criterion without them counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it.
  NO BLUR: D157, D158, D165 and D166 keep their own metrics and cards. Retros score each on its own record: D157 from cycles, D158 from the facilitator checklist, D165 from Intent lines, D166 from slice assumption lines, D167 from slice Inputs lines and the red answers. A D167 catch is not credited to the others. If a D166 assumption line already named a disabled or unknown input, the catch stays with D166, and the card's outcome is tagged to the rule that named it first.
  BASELINE (card 226): 2 input-gap review findings (F1: disabled, unknown and repeated seats; F2: reordered models map), both found after 3 clean greens, about 4 hours after the first red; 3 criteria at 37, 48 and 40 minutes red to green. F3 (unscrubbed evidence) and R1 (a racy guard) are not input-gap findings and do not count.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Delete the slice line and the red answers; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d168"></a>
#### Decider D168: the card 227 retro pick (a slice size cap of 15 KB)

- Date: 2026-11-01. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 227 retro (docs/retros/card-227-2026-10-31.md). Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate and command scans: round 1 2 Yes, 2 Support-it, 2 No, re-run 2 Yes, 2 Support-it, 2 No; one condition adopted (the Size line's self-reference); round 2 6 Yes, 0 Support-it, 0 No, independent re-run 6 Yes, 0 Support-it, 0 No. Copilot seats placed by the gate returned no vote (card 229). Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D168-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D168, the card 227 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: a slice size cap of 15 KB (15360 bytes, measured with wc -c on the slice file at the slice commit); a larger card is split into more slices, each under the cap, each with its own commit. The mid-card review, review after every criterion and the finding read-marker are deferred, not rejected (all eight voices converged on thin slicing, as Dispatch observed).
  Why: card 227's slice was 55 KB, too big for a prompt. Ready to sign-off then took about 14 h: reviews found 27 findings after three clean greens, and seven fix increments (about 7 h) followed. A smaller slice is read whole by the vote window, the seats and the security review, and its criteria are fewer. Card 227 also spawned follow-up card 228 with 11 owned lines, so the cap must not become a way to push scope into follow-up cards; that is the counter-metric to watch.
  Why one lever and not two: the cap plus a mid-card review would be two rules. If findings fell we could not say which one moved them (the D157 and D158 lesson: one change per trial). The cap also needs no new seat or meeting; a review after the first green adds a review window per card. So the mid-card review waits for the D168 trial retro, where the 14 h tail and the findings per card show whether the cap alone was enough.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe a slice file of 15360 bytes or less, with larger cards split into several such slices, cuts late review load and time from ready to first red. We will know when review findings per card fall from 27 toward half that, and slice commit to first red stays at or under 41 minutes.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Every slice file committed for a card is 15360 bytes or fewer. A card whose slice would be larger is split into numbered slices (slice 1 of n), each its own commit, each carrying whole criteria; a criterion is never cut in two. Slice n+1 is committed before the first red that needs it.
  C2. The architect writes "Size: <N> bytes" as the last line of each slice, where N is the byte count of the slice file without that final Size line (everything before it), measured with `head -n -1 <slice file> | wc -c` just before the slice commit. The slice commit body repeats the number.
  C3. The reviewer re-measures at review with the same definition: `git show <slice commit>:<path> | head -n -1 | wc -c`, and compares it with N. A slice whose N exceeds 15360, a slice with no final Size line, or a Size line whose N differs from the re-measure is "not run". Review checks it first.
  C4. A criterion or line moved to a follow-up card to meet the cap is named in the slice commit body ("moved to card N: <what>"), and its owned lines are counted in the retro. No new file, seat or meeting.
  C5. This is not D157's one criterion per red-green cycle (that rule is about red and green order; this one is about slice size). It is not D158's facilitator checklist, D165's intent line, D166's assumption line or D167's hostile-input list. A slice may carry those lines; they count toward the 15360 bytes.
  METRIC: review findings per card (card 227: 27), from the review records; with slice commit to first red in minutes (card 227: 41).
  COUNTER-METRICS: follow-up cards filed and their owned lines (card 227: 228 with 11); slices per card; hours from ready to sign-off (227: about 14 h); full-run minutes. Evidence, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 2 cards that would have had a slice over 15 KB or three or more criteria, the window of D150 to D158 and D165 to D167. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider. Cards with a slice under the cap without splitting do not count.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per card, git shows every slice commit with a Size line, and git show <slice commit>:<path> | wc -c gives 15360 or fewer and matches the Size line. A matching card with an oversize slice, a missing Size line or a mismatch counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it. The checker is the reviewer at review (C3), re-checked by the auditor tick.
  NO BLUR: D157, D158, D165, D166 and D167 keep their own metrics and cards. Retros score each on its own record: D157 from cycles, D158 from the facilitator checklist, D165 from Intent lines, D166 from slice assumption lines, D167 from slice Inputs lines and the red answers, D168 from slice Size lines and the wc -c re-measure. A D168 catch is not credited to the others. A review finding that a D166 or D167 line already named stays with that rule; D168 is credited only for a fall in findings per card and in slice-to-first-red time, and the card's outcome is tagged to the rule that named it first.
  BASELINE (card 227): slice 55 KB (pointer prompt); slice 09:45 to first red 10:26 (41 minutes); filed 08:2x to first red about 2 h; 27 review findings after three clean greens (ten blocking kept, seven lines to card 228); seven fix increments, about 7 h; ready to sign-off about 14 h; follow-up card 228 with 11 owned lines.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Delete the cap and the Size line; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes. Slices already split stay as they are.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.
  ADOPTED CONDITION (round 1, D145; the architect, the QA engineer in both runs and the backend developer in the re-run, one condition in four wordings): the Size number counts the slice file's bytes before its own final Size line, and the reviewer re-measures with that same definition (C2 and C3 above, replacing the self-referencing wording). The re-poll may not add new demands.

<a id="d169"></a>
#### Decider D169: product owner ruling (not a team vote): detection after the fact is acceptable for the deletion-ban corrective, on two conditions

- Date: 2026-11-01 (relayed by the product owner after card 227's sign-off). Context: card 227 ("Recursive deletes on variable paths are blocked and every commit is pushed") was signed off as detected, not blocked (d060208), and asked whether "block" means prevention before a command runs.
- Ruling: detection after the fact is acceptable for this card, on two conditions. (1) Commit and push regularly: the every-commit-pushed rule and the 5-minute unpushed detector stand, and the detector must actually be called, not just written. (2) Recursive deletes never target anything outside the repository working tree. Inside the tree git can restore committed work; outside it (the home folder, ~/.ssh, other repositories, system paths) nothing can be restored.
- Rationale (the product owner's): committed work inside the tree is recoverable, so detect-and-fail is enough there; out-of-tree deletes are the unrecoverable case (the 2026-10-08 wipe). The team decides how to guarantee condition 2; detection alone cannot stop an out-of-tree delete, so the team considers an allow-list of "inside the working tree" rather than "scratch folder only", and a pre-run refusal for out-of-tree deletes (card 228).
- Follow-up requested by the product owner: a detector for uncommitted work older than about 30 minutes in a watched repository, because the push rule does not catch work that was never committed.

<a id="d170"></a>
#### Decider D170: product owner ruling (not a team vote): the indexed process wiki is the highest priority, with a one-card stop-the-line waiver

- CORRECTED by D172 (2026-11-01): the product owner's intent was for the team to EVALUATE an indexed, just-in-time process wiki, not to build it as a mandate; the wording "build" below was a relay error by Dispatch. Card 234 built it before the correction arrived; that build is now a prototype under the evaluation (card 237).

- Date: 2026-11-01. Not a team vote: a product owner priority ruling and an explicit stop-the-line override, the same mechanism as D162 (the one-card waiver for card 219).
- Ruling: card 234 ("An indexed process wiki with just-in-time reading") is the highest priority. It is ranked first at the next re-rank after card 227 closed and starts immediately, ahead of the open-FAIL correctives (cards 228, 210 and 211). The open FAILs stay open and owned, and their correctives resume right after card 234 closes. The waiver covers card 234 only.
- Rationale (the product owner's): it is a process change that improves every card from here on, including how the correctives themselves get done. Every turn re-reads the whole context, so fresh seats overpay by reading everything and the long facilitator session overpays on every turn; the wiki cuts both and gives the product owner one readable place for the whole process.
- Experiment frame: metric tokens per seat (baseline about 50K at setup, about 125K median at the end) and facilitator context size (about 965K); counter-metric rule misses caused by a seat not reading a page it needed. If it passes its keep verdict, the index and the just-in-time pages go to the mob-defaults template through the normal template-change path, including the template review.
- Reconciliation with stop-the-line: the product owner asked that a conflict be decided by Decider and recorded; the product owner's explicit override settles it, as with D162 (a Decider cannot waive the product owner's own stop-the-line rule, D160). The audit sees it as authorized, not drift.

<a id="d171"></a>
#### Decider D171: the card 234 retro pick (cite-a-point votes)

- Date: 2026-11-01. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 234 retro (docs/retros/card-234-2026-11-01.md), on Dispatch's queued proposal. Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate and command scans: round 1 5 Yes, 1 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 4 Yes, 2 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D171-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  Decider D171, the card 234 retro process pick, as an experiment
  PICK: cite-a-point votes (Dispatch's queued proposal; the product manager, the delivery lead and in part the backend developer): every vote carries one line quoting a point from the proposal text, found in git; a vote with no such line is an unread vote and does not count. "No number without a committed source" and parallel vote launch are deferred, not rejected.
  Why: card 227's stand-down found 3 of 7 voters had not read the slice, and a vote that was not read decides nothing. On card 234 the two windows took 36 and 45 minutes, and Copilot decision seats returned no window line in 14 of 14 slice-window and 7 of 7 task-force attempts, so a silent seat could not be told from a reader. A quoted point is the cheapest evidence of reading, and git can check it.
  Why this cluster and not the source cluster: the architect, reviewer and in part QA want every number traced to a committed source. Card 234 already built that check for its own numbers (fix increments 1b to 3b: every number traced to a committed source; the real restart checked against a committed key), so for card 234 the rule is in force and adds nothing. Beyond card 234 it means changing the Proof block checker, which needs a card and a D-number cited in its subject (D145 C2); that is a checker change, not a small process rule. Take it to its own card once the checker's false-positive rate on card 234 is known.
  Why not parallel launch now: it changes how decider.py launches seats, and window minutes is this rule's counter-metric. Both together would blur what moved the minutes (the D157 and D158 lesson: one change per trial). It is the next pick after the D171 trial retro.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe requiring one verifiable cited point per vote cuts votes cast without reading. We will know when unread or uncited votes per window fall from 3 of 7 (card 227) to 0, with window time not rising more than 5 minutes.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Each vote reply ends with one line "CITE: <point>", after the last Pn line. The point is either a quoted phrase of at least 6 words copied exactly from the proposal body, or "line N: <first 4 words of that line>". The title line and the VOTING RULE line do not count as a point.
  C2. Check in git: the window commit holds the proposal file. The reviewer runs `git show <window commit>:<path> | grep -F -- "<phrase>"`, or for a line reference `git show <window commit>:<path> | sed -n 'Np'` and compares the 4 words. No match, a line past the end, or no CITE line: the vote is unread.
  C3. An unread vote does not count in the tally, for any of Yes, Support-it or No. The facilitator re-asks that seat once in the same window. A second unread reply is no vote, and the seat is replaced by the next in least-recently-used order. A seat that returns no window line is also no vote.
  C4. The check is done by hand and by grep. No change to decider.py, a checker or a seat list. No new file, seat or meeting.
  C5. This is not D168's slice size cap, D167's hostile-input list, D166's assumption line, D165's intent line, D158's facilitator checklist or D157's one criterion per cycle. The CITE line is in the vote, not in the slice or the red.
  METRIC: unread or uncited votes per window, from the votes file and the grep check (card 227: 3 of 7; target 0).
  COUNTER-METRICS: minutes per window (card 234: 36 and 45); re-asks per window; no-window-line seats; CITE lines that quote only the PICK or title (ceremony). Evidence, not targets. A quote shows the text was seen, not understood; this is a floor on reading, not a measure of it.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 vote windows of any kind (task-force, slice or Decider; a re-run counts with its window), the window of D150 to D158 and D165 to D168. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per window, git shows the proposal at the window commit and the votes file with one CITE line per vote, and each CITE line is found by the C2 grep or sed. A window with a vote counted without a found CITE line counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it. The checker is the reviewer at review, re-checked by the auditor tick.
  NO BLUR: D157, D158 and D165 to D168 keep their own metrics and cards. Retros score each on its own record: D157 from cycles, D158 from the facilitator checklist, D165 from Intent lines, D166 from slice assumption lines, D167 from slice Inputs lines and red answers, D168 from slice Size lines, D171 from votes CITE lines. A D171 catch is not credited to the others. D171 is credited only for a fall in unread votes per window; a slice finding stays with the slice rule that named it.
  BASELINE (card 234): task-force window 03:05 to 03:41 (36 minutes), slice window 03:50 to 04:35 (45 minutes), each with one condition adopted; 21 of 21 Copilot decision-seat attempts returned no window line; unread votes on card 234 were not counted. Card 227 stand-down: 3 of 7 voters had not read the slice.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Drop the CITE line; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes. Votes already counted stay as recorded.
  VOTING RULE: reply first line Yes, Support-it or No, then P1: to Pn: one line each (one per proposal point). A No names ONE fixed condition (CONDITION: ...). A reasonable, reversible condition is adopted and the proposal re-polled; a later round may not add new demands. Only an irreconcilable purpose or model conflict escalates. Independent fresh-seat re-run before recording.

<a id="d172"></a>
#### Decider D172: product owner correction (not a team vote): the indexed process wiki is to be evaluated, not built as a mandate

- Date: 2026-11-01. Not a team vote: a product owner correction of D170, relayed after card 234 had closed.
- Correction: the product owner's intent was for the team to EVALUATE the idea. D170's "build" wording was a relay error by Dispatch, not the product owner's intent. The evaluation (card 237): the team assesses whether an indexed, just-in-time process wiki actually solves the problem (tokens per seat, facilitator context growth, messages blocked by long turns); compares alternatives, including doing nothing; estimates the cost; and decides by Decider whether to adopt it, reshape it or reject it.
- Priority: the top priority and the one-card stop-the-line waiver stay, and now apply to the evaluation itself (card 237), not to a build. If the team decides to build, that becomes its own card, ranked by the team. The defaults promotion (template) applies only if a later build passes.
- What exists: card 234 built a wiki before this correction (docs/process-wiki/, its checkers and briefs; experiment verdict REVISE, 39d0c9e). It stands as a prototype and evidence for the evaluation, not as an adopted rule.
- Proposal folded in (product owner proposal, not a directive): at the end of every retro, the team reviews the wiki and records additions, updates and removals, with evidence (a wiki commit or an explicit "no changes" note). The evaluation decides it by Decider, as an experiment if adopted. Metric: wiki staleness (rules changed in retros but not reflected in the wiki). Counter-metric: added retro time. The team may adopt, reshape or reject it.

<a id="d173"></a>
#### Decider D173: card 237 evaluation outcome: RESHAPE the indexed process wiki; retro-end wiki review as a three-retro experiment

- Date: 2026-11-01. Proposer: product manager seat (ready), .mob/records/237/assessment-pm.md (d2ad79f), from the independent reviewer's recompute (.mob/records/237/turn-recompute-reviewer.md, 85ea412) of stage A (12 runs, ledger 0e6cb7d corrected in ca56511; trial checker green). Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D173-votes.json.
- Motion A result: PASSED (round 1 6 Yes, 0 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run 5 Yes, 1 Support-it, 0 No). Adopted text: Card 237 outcome: RESHAPE. The stage A result is accepted as the independent reviewer recomputed it (median token delta 41.74%, five of six roles at or over 20%; one wiki miss against zero baseline misses, caused by the thin-slice limits being absent from the slice route; blocked messages vacuous at 0 and 0; upkeep and facilitator context growth not measured). Adopt is not permitted by the committed thresholds (one wiki miss; upkeep unmeasured) and reject is not (the excess is 1, not 2). No threshold or cap changes. Stage B is not run now. The team orders one follow-up card, ranked by the team, to put the thin-slice limits on the slice route, measure upkeep against the committed budget, add a staleness check, and re-run the slice cell under the same thresholds and an independent recompute. No build is ordered by this motion.
- Motion B result: PASSED (round 1 2 Yes, 4 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run 1 Yes, 5 Support-it, 0 No). Adopted text: Retro-end wiki review: RESHAPE and adopt as a time-boxed experiment, only if motion A passes. It runs on the reshaped wiki for a number of retros the Decider names (proposed: the next three). At each retro-end the team records the output of a staleness check (rules changed in the retro but not reflected in the wiki) and the wiki commit it caused, or an explicit 'no changes' note. Metric: wiki staleness. Counter-metric: added retro time, held to the committed 10-minute upkeep line. It ends early if the line is passed, and lapses if motion A fails. The experiment covers the next three retros after the follow-up card lands, as proposed.
- Follows D172: the evaluation is decided; no build is ordered. The defaults promotion stays conditional on a later build passing.

<a id="d174"></a>
#### Decider D174: the card 237 retro pick (parallel voter dispatch)

- Date: 2026-11-01. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 237 retro (docs/retros/card-237-2026-11-01.md), on Dispatch's queued proposal. Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate: round 1 1 Yes, 5 Support-it, 0 No (the decider's first reply re-asked once under D171); independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 3 Yes, 3 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D174-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  PICK: parallel voter dispatch for every vote window. The facilitator probes once, assigns the whole rotation up front, writes every seat-use line in one commit, then launches all voters of the window at the same time. (Proposed by the QA engineer and agile coach voices; the architect and decider voices named the serial windows as a team cost; Dispatch queued it.) The cap estimated from a measured pilot run, the git-checkable brief preflight and live-card context measurement are deferred, not rejected.
  Why: since the decision-seat gate landed, voters run one at a time. On card 237 the five decision windows each took about 10 to 14 minutes, and the threshold confirmation and the two rounds of Decider D173 ran the same way. The retro's own seven voices started one after another from 18:28:59 to 18:37:52, about 9 minutes before the last one began. Before the gate a window took about 3 minutes; on card 234 the task-force and slice windows took 36 and 45 minutes. The slowdown is a side effect of the gate, not a choice. Dispatch expects parallel launch to make votes more independent and to cut same-checkout write collisions (card 230); card 237 recorded one overlap of two decision runs on the shared seat-use history, which caused no refusal but could.
  Why this one and not the others: it is the only candidate with a measured baseline on every window of the card, a before-the-gate figure to compare with, and counter-metrics that already exist in the record. D171 named it as the next pick after the cite-a-point trial retro; that trial is now complete, so the blur that held it back (window minutes were D171's counter-metric) no longer applies. The cap estimate cost one stretch of about 2 hours (about 14:20 to 16:24) on one card, the cap did its job, and the measured figure now exists (8,868,059 tokens over 12 runs, about 739,000 per run), so card 238's slice can use it without a new rule. The brief preflight bundles three levers (cap, cite check, no-commit marker), and the D157 and D158 lesson is one change per trial. Live-card context measurement is already part of card 238 (upkeep and facilitator context growth are in D173 motion A).
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe probing once, assigning the whole rotation up front, writing the seat-use lines in one commit and launching all voters in parallel cuts the time of a vote window back toward 3 minutes without costing fairness or independence. We will know when the median minutes per vote window fall from 10 to 14 (card 237) to 5 or fewer over the trial, with no least-recently-used violation.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Before a vote window opens, the facilitator checks that the Codex, Copilot, Grok and agy probes are under 60 minutes old (the standing rule, F-040) and re-probes first if any is older. Probe evidence is committed as today.
  C2. The facilitator assigns every voter of the window in least-recently-used order in one step, and writes all the seat-use lines together with the proposal file in one window commit. A seat that is disabled or unknown at that point is skipped as today; the skip is recorded in that commit.
  C3. After the window commit, all voters are launched at the same time, each in its own fresh context through the decision-seat gate. No voter sees another voter's reply. The existing per-seat timeout stays; a seat that returns nothing by the timeout is no vote. The window closes when every seat has replied or timed out.
  C4. D171 stays as it is, whatever its trial verdict: each vote still carries its CITE line, and an unread vote is re-asked or replaced as D171 C3 says. Re-asks run in parallel as a second batch; a replacement seat is the next in least-recently-used order and its seat-use line goes in one follow-up commit.
  C5. The rule "no commit while a voter runs" stands and binds seats as well as the facilitator (card 237 observation). The launcher must not write the shared seat-use history from two runs at the same time; if it cannot be made safe, that window falls back to serial launch and the trial records it as a fallback, not as a pass.
  C6. This is not D171's cite-a-point rule, D168's slice size cap or D167's hostile-input list. It changes only when and how voters start. The proposal text, the voting rule, the number of voters and the quorum are unchanged. No new file, seat or meeting.
  C7. Copilot seats placed by the gate that return no reply (card 229) are handled as today: no vote, replaced in least-recently-used order. This pick does not try to fix card 229.
  METRIC: minutes per vote window, from the window commit to the last counted vote, taken from the votes file timestamps and the commit times (card 237: about 10 to 14 each; card 234: 36 and 45; before the gate: about 3). Target: median 5 or fewer.
  COUNTER-METRICS: least-recently-used violations per window (target 0, from the seat-use lines); stale-probe launches per window (a probe over 60 minutes old; target 0); unread or uncited votes per window (must not rise above the D171 rate; card 237: 4 seats with an unread first reply, 5 unread replies, across five windows, the threshold confirmation and D173); same-checkout write collisions or gate refusals per window (card 237: 1 overlap, 0 refusals); fallbacks to serial launch. Evidence, not targets, except the first two.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 vote windows of any kind (task-force, slice or Decider; a re-run counts with its window), the window of D150 to D158 and D165 to D171. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: per window, git shows one window commit that holds the proposal and every seat-use line before the first voter result, and the votes file shows every seat's start time within a few seconds of the others. A window launched one voter at a time, or with seat-use lines written after a voter started, counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it. The checker is the reviewer at review, re-checked by the auditor tick.
  NO BLUR: D157, D158, D165 to D168 and D171 keep their own metrics and cards. Retros score each on its own record: D171 from votes CITE lines, this pick from window minutes and the seat-use commits. A fall in minutes is credited here; a fall in unread votes stays with D171.
  BASELINE (card 237): five decision windows (237-taskforce, 237-slice, 237-key-secrecy, 237-cap-fired, 237-amendment-ordering) at about 10 to 14 minutes each with voters one at a time; the decider threshold confirmation and the two-round D173 on the same footing; one overlap of two decision runs on the shared seat-use history; Copilot returned no reply in every window (card 229). Card 234: 36 and 45 minutes. Before the gate: about 3.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Go back to launching voters one at a time; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes. Votes already counted stay as recorded.

<a id="d175"></a>
#### Decider D175: the card 228 retro pick (a written cycle cap per criterion in every slice)

- Date: 2026-11-02. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 228 retro (docs/retros/card-228-2026-11-02.md). Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate: round 1 4 Yes, 2 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 4 Yes, 2 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D175-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  PICK: every slice carries a written Cap line for each acceptance criterion, and every item of a slice's hostile-input list is labelled SEEN or INSURANCE. A cycle past a criterion's cap needs a scope window adopted before its red starts, not after. (Proposed by the architect voice; the sign-off seat put forward the same pick; the stand-down 1 verdict was "tune" from four of seven voices.) The test-impact lever, the single decider for reversible parameters, the ruling-as-red-test rule, the bypass corpus and the cards for re-rank 18 are deferred or routed elsewhere, not rejected.
  User benefit: the product owner and the team learn the maximum cost of a card before it starts, not from the auditor after the fact. Card 228 cost about 13 hours and twelve increments plus a fix, against a three-increment rule and a slice that said one cycle per criterion.
  Why this one: it answers the largest measured cost of the card, and the only one the team can change inside the slice.
  - The slice (639c5e2, 14,847 bytes) listed hostile forms with no cap. Criterion 1 ran seven cycles (1a to 1g) against a slice of one, from 12:51 to 17:54 local, about 5 hours of the 13. 1g was born green and added guards, not behaviour. Criterion 2 ran three, criterion 3 two, and a fix cycle followed: twelve increments plus a fix against D136's three.
  - No cap was written before the overrun. The auditor's FLAG caught the first; the later two were decided at the boundary they hit (window 228-c2-scope, stand-down 1, window 228-gate-fix).
  - The forms were never priced. No record counts how many of the forms of 1a to 1f have appeared in a real seat transcript: the gate re-ran the scanner on 25 real transcripts (627 Bash calls) and found no real delete, only false positives. If none, the extra increments were insurance.
  - Every voice's view of stand-down 1 points here: tune from the PM-ready, QA, audit-lens and decider voices; keep from the architect, developer and coach. The architect's candidate is this pick; the sign-off asks for the same cap line and the same SEEN/INSURANCE split.
  - It extends two rules that exist (D136's increment limit, D167's hostile-input list) and adds no gate, seat, file or meeting (issue 245's test: prefer extending a mechanism).
  Why not the others: the test-impact lever (two voices) is a tooling change and the commit-then-suite rule from green 3a already removed the commit wait; the single decider and parallel windows belong to a decider.py card (issue 247) and a standing-rule Decider; the ruling-as-red-test rule rests on one event the gate caught. Details in the retro, section 5.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that writing the increment cap per criterion into the slice, and labelling each hostile form SEEN or INSURANCE, keeps a card inside the cycles its slice promised and makes extra cycles an explicit decision made before the red, without pushing the risk into later cards. We will know when, over the trial, no card runs a red past its Cap line without a scope window adopted before that red, and the counter-metrics do not rise.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. The architect's slice carries, under each acceptance criterion, one line "Cap: criterion n, at most k cycles". The k values sum to at most three for the card (D136); a card that needs more is split (D168). A fix cycle after the gate counts against the card's total and is named in the window that adopts it.
  C2. A slice with a hostile-input list (D167) labels each item SEEN or INSURANCE. SEEN carries a pointer: a commit, a seat transcript id, an audit or an incident that shows the form. INSURANCE means no real occurrence is known. Forms the cap cannot cover go to a named follow-up row in the same slice, as the card 228 slice did with F1 to F6.
  C3. The reviewer who reads the slice (D168) rejects a slice with a criterion that has no Cap line, a Cap line sum over three, or a list item with no label. A slice that fails is re-written; no red starts on it.
  C4. A red that would be cycle k+1 or later for its criterion starts only after a scope window (Decider) has adopted it. That window is committed before the red. Its proposal states the cycles used and the hours used, the forms the extra cycle covers with their labels, and what a stop would leave open. A hostile form found during a cycle that is not in the slice goes to the follow-up rows unless such a window adopts it (the rule window 228-c1-scope wrote after the fact).
  C5. The red turn record opens with one line "Cycle i of k for criterion n", copied from the Cap line. Reds already name "Criterion: n" in the commit message, so the count can be read from git.
  C6. During the trial the facilitator adds C1 to C5 to the architect's and the QA engineer's briefs. Only a keep verdict goes to the mob-defaults template, through the normal template-change path including the template review.
  C7. Not changed: D136's other limits (400 lines, 15 files, three criteria), D167's requirement to list hostile inputs, D168's 15360-byte slice cap (the new lines count toward it), D151 stand-downs, the green commit-then-suite rule, D171 and D174. No new meeting, seat, file or gate. This pick does not change what a scan covers or how many forms the scanner must handle.
  METRIC: reds past a criterion's Cap line, per card, that start without a scope window committed before them (card 228: criterion 1 had 6 beyond a slice of one, criterion 2 had 2 beyond one; every one was decided after the overrun began or at the boundary it hit). Target: 0. Read from the red commit messages ("Criterion: n"), the slice's Cap lines and the scope window commit times. Context figure, not a target: increments per card (card 228: twelve plus a fix; three allowed).
  COUNTER-METRICS: pre-ship gate HOLDs per card (card 228: 1, the ssh-agent bypass); follow-up cards or rows opened per card (card 228: six, F1 to F6, fifteen items); hours from first card commit to sign-off (card 228: about 13); scope windows adopted per card and how many were refused (card 228: three, none refused; if every window is adopted the cap is theatre); a delete form or false-positive class found after the card closes that the slice labelled INSURANCE and left to a follow-up. A cap met by sending the risk to later cards would show in the second and fifth. Evidence for the trial retro, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 cards whose slice is written after this Decider is recorded, of any kind; a card with a split slice counts once. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: at slice read, the reviewer checks one Cap line per criterion, a sum of at most three, and a label on each list item. At the pre-ship gate, the independent reviewer counts reds per criterion from "Criterion: n" against the Cap lines and lists each red past a cap with the commit of the window that adopted it. A red past a cap whose window was committed after it counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it. The checker is the reviewer, re-checked by the auditor.
  NO BLUR: stand-down 1 of card 228 (two cycles per remaining criterion) was a one-card adjustment; its verdict stays in the card 228 retro. D136, D167 and D168 keep their own metrics. This pick is scored on reds past a cap, not on card duration; a fall in duration is credited only if the counter-metrics hold.
  BASELINE (card 228): slice of one cycle per criterion with no Cap lines; criterion 1 seven cycles (1a to 1g), criterion 2 three (2a to 2c), criterion 3 two (3a, 3b), one gate-fix cycle; windows 228-c1-scope, 228-c2-scope and 228-gate-fix at 15.5, 18.6 and 16.8 minutes; about 13 hours first commit to sign-off; one gate HOLD; six follow-up cards unfiled at sign-off.
  COST (issue 245): about one line per criterion and one label per list item in the slice, one line in each red turn record, and a scope window only when a cycle is added. No extra seat or meeting. If the cost shows in the trial it is the first thing the retro cuts.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Drop the lines from the briefs; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes. Slices already written keep their lines or lose them with no effect.

<a id="d176"></a>
#### Decider D176: the card 210 retro pick (gate reports state their environment and check untouched failures in the reference shell)

- Date: 2026-11-02. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 210 retro (docs/retros/card-210-2026-11-02.md). Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate: round 1 4 Yes, 2 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 4 Yes, 2 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D176-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  PICK: every pre-ship gate report opens each test run with an Environment line (the shell and how it was launched, the working directory, the commit hash), and every failing test in a module that no commit of the card touches is re-run, failing modules only, in the reference shell, with its own Environment line, in the same report and before the verdict. The reference shell is the one in which the card's greens and the facilitator recorded their full-suite results; the facilitator's gate brief names it. (Proposed by the architect voice, shell and repo provenance for every gate run, and the audit-lens voice, a normalised recipe in the report; the decider voice and the sign-off seat ask for the same plain-shell run.) The line-ending check, the Copilot decision-seat fix and a circuit breaker, parallel windows, test-impact analysis and the refactor proposal are deferred or routed to cards, not rejected.
  User benefit: the product owner gets one gate verdict per card that does not have to be reversed. Card 210's HOLD was overturned 43 minutes later by the same code, about 15 percent of the card's 4 hours 45 minutes, and cost a second 25 minute full-suite run by the facilitator and 8 minutes 26 seconds of a re-check seat.
  Why this one: it is the largest avoidable cost on this card that the team can change, and it is the cheapest.
  - The gate started at 15:40 UTC and the HOLD (d20314c) landed at 16:14: two failures in tests/test_mob_summary.py, from a 1545-test run of 1520 seconds. The re-check (3d03129) returned SHIP at 16:57 because the same tests pass in a plain bash shell (1545 OK, 528b3d9) and no card 210 commit touches them. From the HOLD to the SHIP was 43 minutes; the gate and its re-check took 36 minutes 50 seconds of Copilot seat time.
  - The first report named no shell. The record learned it afterwards: PowerShell into cmd /c. The sign-off says a rule that the gate states the shell it runs the suite in would have saved one of the two runs. The gate's evidence for "plain shell passes" is the facilitator's run, not its own, which the sign-off lists as a weakness.
  - The HOLD was not wrong. The failure is a real portability bug (card 268: the tests pass in one shell and fail in another), and only a seat in a different environment could find it. The pick does not try to stop that finding; it asks the report to carry both results so the verdict does not need a second round.
  - Five of seven voices named the gate's environment in some form: the architect (shell and environment provenance), the audit lens (a git-only reproducibility packet with a normalised shell recipe), the decider (run the gate in a plain shell too), the coach (no Copilot CLI suite runs until card 268) and the QA engineer (replace the gate seat). Their wordings differ; the common lever is "say where it ran, and check a failure where the others ran".
  - It extends the gate's existing report and the brief the facilitator already writes. It adds no gate, seat, file or meeting (issue 245's test).
  Why not the others: the line-ending check (QA engineer, decider, developer and audit-lens voices) answers one event that sign-off caught, and bug card 269 (restore LF or add .gitattributes) may remove the need for any check; if it recurs it is the next candidate. The circuit breaker (product manager and coach voices; issue 242 point 2) and parallel windows (card 265) are decider.py changes, and the Copilot decision-seat fix (card 229) is ranked third by re-rank 18 and removes the cause. Test-impact analysis (developer voice) is a tooling build. The refactor-step proposal (issue 260) is a standing-rule change for its own Decider. Details in the retro, section 5.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that a gate report that states where each test ran and shows a failing module's result in the reference shell reaches its final verdict in one report, instead of a HOLD and a re-check, when a failure comes from the gate seat's environment, without letting a real failure through. We will know when, over the trial, no HOLD is overturned for environment, no defect the gate passed is found later in its scope, and the counter-metrics do not rise.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. The facilitator's gate brief names the reference shell from the card record: the shell in which the greens' and the facilitator's full-suite results were recorded (card 210: plain bash, 1475, 1517 and 1545 OK), and how to start it. The gate seat runs the full suite there when it can.
  C2. Before each test result the gate report carries one Environment line: the shell and how it was launched (for example "Copilot CLI direct", "PowerShell into cmd /c", "bash -lc"), the working directory, the output of git rev-parse HEAD, the command, the test count and the seconds. A run without that line is not counted as evidence.
  C3. On a failing test the gate lists the failing test ids and shows by git whether any commit of the card touches the failing module or what it calls (as the card 210 re-check did with git log and git show --stat). If none does, it re-runs the failing modules only, not the full suite, in the reference shell, and writes the result with an Environment line in the same report before the verdict.
  C4. A gate seat that cannot start the reference shell says "environment-unverified" and the verdict is HOLD. The facilitator then runs the same failing modules in the reference shell and records that as the facilitator's run in the same report file, not as the gate's.
  C5. Verdict rules otherwise unchanged. A failure that fails in both shells, or in a module the card touches, is a HOLD. A failure that fails only in the gate's shell, passes in the reference shell in the gate's own report and is touched by no card commit is written "environment-dependent"; the facilitator files a bug card at once (card 268's form) and the gate may SHIP with that bug listed. A gate that did not itself see the reference-shell pass may not SHIP on it.
  C6. Not changed: the independent reviewer owns the verdict; the full suite still runs once at the gate; sign-off and the auditor; D175's cap rule, D168, D171, D174 and the green commit-then-suite rule; card 268's own fix. No new meeting, seat, file or gate. During the trial the facilitator adds C1 to C5 to the gate brief and the sign-off checklist. Only a keep verdict goes to the mob-defaults template, through the normal template-change path including the template review.
  METRIC: HOLDs overturned for environment, per gate: a HOLD followed by a SHIP on the same code because the failing tests pass in the reference shell and no card commit touches them. Read from the gate files and the commit times. Card 210: 1 (HOLD d20314c 16:14 UTC, SHIP 3d03129 16:57 UTC, 43 minutes, one extra 25 minute suite run, 8 minutes 26 seconds of re-check seat). Target: 0 over the next 3 gates. Context figure, not a target: minutes from the first gate verdict to the final one (card 210: 43). A gate with no failing test gives no reading on this metric and is counted for C2 only.
  COUNTER-METRICS: (1) defects the gate report passed that sign-off, the auditor or a later card finds inside the gate's scope (card 210: the line-ending rewrite in green 3, found by sign-off, which this pick does not cover; tests: 0); a gate that SHIPs past a real red would show here. (2) HOLDs that are real, failing in both shells or in a module the card touches (card 210: 0); a gate that never HOLDs across three cards is a reading to set against (1). (3) Gate seat minutes per gate (card 210: 28 minutes 24 seconds first run, 8 minutes 26 seconds re-check; the failing module alone took 193.6 seconds direct and 188.5 seconds through cmd /c), the cost of C3. (4) Open environment-dependent bug cards at the end of the trial and days open (card 268 was filed at the gate and open at sign-off); if failures are shipped past and left open, the cost has moved to the backlog. Evidence for the trial retro, not targets.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 pre-ship gates whose brief is written after this Decider is recorded, of any seat or model; a gate with a re-check counts once. If card 268 lands first and no failure appears, the trial reads C2 only, and the trial retro may propose a longer run by its own Decider. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: at the sign-off read, the sign-off seat checks that each test result in the gate report has an Environment line, that the reference shell is the one the brief named, and that each failing module has its reference-shell result or the words "environment-unverified". The facilitator checks the hash in each line against the card's HEAD in git. A report missing any of these counts as not run; the auditor tick may FAIL it. The checker is the sign-off seat, re-checked by the auditor.
  NO BLUR: D175 (cycle caps), D168, D171 and D174 keep their own metrics; a HOLD caused by a cap breach is not scored here. This pick is scored on HOLDs overturned for environment, not on card duration. A fix to card 268 that removes the failure is credited to card 268, not to this pick. The line-ending rewrite stays with bug card 269.
  BASELINE (card 210): one gate HOLD (two failures in tests/test_mob_summary.py; 1545 tests in 1520 seconds in the gate's PowerShell-into-cmd shell), a re-check SHIP, the facilitator's plain-bash full run 1545 OK; a first report with no shell statement; 43 minutes from HOLD to SHIP; 36 minutes 50 seconds of gate-seat time; no Environment lines.
  COST (issue 245): one brief line (C1), one Environment line per test run, and one extra module run when a test fails (about 3 minutes in card 210's case, none when the suite is green). No extra seat or meeting. If the cost shows in the trial it is the first thing the retro cuts.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Remove the lines from the gate brief and the sign-off checklist; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes. C5 reverts to "HOLD on any red suite".

Not a team vote: a product owner ruling on a blocker card 211's ready seat and slice named (all 27 commits 687410e..e3736d1 and every seat commit under one identity, Xipooo <xipooo@gmail.com>, so a review "committed by an identity that authored none of the 27 commits" cannot be met by seat or context rotation).
