<a id="d178"></a>
#### Decider D178: the card 211 retro pick (a breaker for decision-seat rotation)

- Date: 2026-11-03. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 211 retro (docs/retros/card-211-2026-11-02.md), on the product owner's queued circuit-breaker proposal. Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate: round 1 0 Yes, 6 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 0 Yes, 6 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D178-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  PICK: decision-seat rotation gets a breaker. A model trips when two decision-seat replies in a row (any windows) are empty or carry no vote word, or when its CLI reports its quota reached (one event is enough). A tripped model is placed in no decision seat; a quota trip also keeps it out of every other rotation-placed seat, including retro voices. It returns when a probe vote parses. Proposed by five of seven voices (product manager, architect, developer, audit lens, coach; issue 242 point 2, the queued rotation input). A per-model budget, a stricter vote-cite rule, a pin of the final-state review to HEAD and the decision-seat fixes (cards 229, 270) are deferred or already cards, not rejected.
  User benefit: the product owner gets decision windows that spend their minutes on votes and not on seats known to be silent. Card 211's two windows placed 19 empty Grok, Copilot and agy attempts (9 and 10); in the slice window about 2 minutes 10 seconds of each 3.5 minute seat went on the two empties before Claude answered (from the attempt start times).
  Why this one: it is the largest recurring waste on this card, and the one the most voices named.
  - Window 211-taskforce 19:19 to 19:42 UTC (22.5 minutes): 9 empty attempts (Grok 4, Copilot 4, agy 1), 5 counted votes (agy 1, Claude 4). Window 211-slice 19:52 to 20:10 (17.8 minutes): 10 empty (Grok 5, Copilot 5), 5 counted, all Claude. Card 210's windows: Copilot 0 of 6 placed seats. The Decider: all six votes Claude.
  - Rotation burned agy: about 28 calls in 21 hours until "Individual quota reached" at 19:44 UTC (resets in about 145 hours), because least-recently-used rotation re-seated onto agy each time Grok or Copilot returned nothing. The architect seat had to be re-seated on Copilot.
  - Grok and Copilot are silent only on the decision-seat path. In this retro Grok (3) and Copilot (2) answered as voices, and Copilot's CLI answered ready, slice, review and gate. So the breaker is per path: it must not take the models out of the seats where they work.
  - The breaker cannot create model diversity; it stops paying for its absence. Diversity returns when cards 229 and 270 land and a probe parses.
  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that a breaker on decision-seat rotation removes the empty attempts a window pays for, and so shortens windows, without hiding a fixed model from the rotation for longer than one probe interval. We will know when empty or voteless counted attempts fall from 9 and 10 per window to 0, probe empties stay at most one per tripped model per day, and window minutes do not rise.
  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Trip. Counted per model over seats placed through decider.py (task-force, slice, re-rank, Decider and pick windows). Empty is no text after the CLI call; voteless is no first-line vote word the window accepts. A reply with a vote word is a reply whatever the vote: a No or OBJECTION never trips. Two in a row trips; "quota reached" in the CLI text trips at once.
  C2. Effect. Rotation skips a tripped model; the seat goes to the next least-recently-used model not tripped. A quota trip applies to every rotation-placed seat, including retro voices; an empty trip applies to decision seats only. Seats not placed by rotation (ready, review, gate, sign-off on the Copilot CLI) are unchanged.
  C3. Return. A tripped model gets one probe vote, before a window opens, at most once per 24 hours, or when a fix card for it lands (229, 270). The probe is a fixed one-line proposal sent through the same decider.py path, not counted as a vote. The model returns when its first line parses. A quota trip is probed only after the reset the CLI message names (agy: about 2026-11-08).
  C4. Record. The window's seat-use lines carry one "Breaker:" line per trip or return: model, trigger, time, and for a quota trip the calls since the model's last reset, counted from the seat-use lines. That number is what a per-model budget would need.
  C5. Starting state. Grok and Copilot tripped for decision seats (the record above; cards 229, 270). agy tripped for quota until its reset. The facilitator applies C1 to C4 by hand during the trial; the decider.py change is a follow-up card.
  C6. Not changed: D168, D171, D174, D175, the counted-vote rules, seat roles, sign-off and the auditor. No new seat, meeting or file.
  METRIC: empty or voteless counted attempts per decision window. Baseline: 9 (211-taskforce), 10 (211-slice), 6 over card 210's two windows. Target: 0 in each of the next 3 windows. A probe's empty reply is counted apart, at most one per tripped model per 24 hours. Context figure, not a target: minutes per window (22.5, 17.8; D174's parallel launch, card 265, is a separate lever).
  COUNTER-METRICS: (1) share of counted votes from a model other than Claude (card 211: 1 of 10 window votes, Decider 0 of 6; card 210: 5 of 10 from agy). It will read near 0 while all three are tripped; it is a reading, set against model diversity, not a target. (2) False trips: a tripped model whose first probe parses and whose earlier empties were transient. (3) Days between a fix landing (229, 270) and the model's return. (4) Calls per day to a model that returns after a quota trip (agy), to feed the budget candidate. (5) Window minutes must not rise.
  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 decision windows after this Decider is recorded, of any category. If every model is untripped by then, there is no reading and the trial retro says so. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.
  ACCOUNTABILITY CHECK: at sign-off, the sign-off seat checks each window record: no attempt on a model tripped at that time unless a "Breaker:" return line precedes it, and a probe line for each probe. The auditor re-checks the same from the window json; a window failing it counts as not run.
  NO BLUR: fixes to cards 229 and 270 are credited to those cards, not to this pick. Window minutes also move with D174 and card 265; they are reported, not scored. D171's cite counts are scored by D171.
  COST (issue 245): one Breaker line per trip or return, and at most one probe per tripped model per day (one short prompt; an empty one costs about 1 to 2 minutes). No extra seat, file or meeting. If the cost shows, the probe is the first thing the retro cuts.
  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Stop writing the lines and place seats as before; no shared state, checker or earlier D-record changes.

<a id="d181"></a>
#### Decider D181: the card 276 retro pick (a lettered red ends at the first passing green)

- Date: 2026-11-03. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 276 retro (docs/retros/card-276-2026-11-03.md). Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate: round 1 3 Yes, 3 Support-it, 0 No (the decider seat counted on a D171 re-ask after its first cite dropped words); independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 4 Yes, 2 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D181-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  PICK: a lettered red (4b, 4c) is allowed only before any green has passed the red it corrects. Once a green has passed, the next red for that criterion is a new cycle, counts against the D175 cap, and needs a D175 scope window adopted before it. Proposed by five of seven voices (product manager, QA, developer, decider, coach); the sign-off asks for it (signoff-ctx6.md section 3); the architect voice names it as the one rule to keep. The ledger and the pre-check are deferred.

  User benefit: the product owner learns that a card is past its cap before the extra cycle is spent, not from an audit afterwards. Card 276 spent cycles 5 and 6 with no window; scope-4 ratified them after green 0000715 and refactor fdfa821 had landed (cycle_count_ruling).

  Why this one:
  - It closes the loophole that broke D175 on this card. D175 C4 already says a red past the cap starts only after a window; lettering let reds 25d50b8 and 58b5c26 look like cycle 4 after green 2e56d9f had passed red 5b3292e (cycle_count_ruling). The commit bodies still say "Cycle 4 of 4 ... red amendment 4b/4c" (signoff-ctx6.md section 3).
  - It writes down a line the facilitator already drew: 1b and 3b were in-cycle because no green had passed (cycle_count_ruling). D157 C2 pairs each red with its green, so a passed green closes that pair.
  - Architect's ledger with automatic checks: a tool, and a ledger still needs this definition to count lettered reds right. It is the follow-up once the rule holds.
  - Audit lens's launchability pre-check: it targets empty seat attempts, which are the cmd.exe line limit (proposal 278) and cards 229, 270, 277, not this card's largest process miss.
  - Queued proposals 278 and 284 go to re-rank 20, not to this pick (retro-input.md, queued inputs).

  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that ending a lettered red at the first passing green makes every red past a cap visible before it starts, so D175 windows come before the red and not after. We will know when reds past the cap with no prior window fall from 2 to 0 over the trial, while scope windows per card and the time from a failing real-evidence check to the next red do not rise.

  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Passed green. A green has passed a red when a commit for the same criterion, after that red, is committed as its green with the red's tests passing on its named run. A later failure on real evidence (trimmed fixture, real log) does not reopen it: green 2e56d9f counts as passed.
  C2. Lettered red. A red may carry a letter (nb) only if no green has passed red n. Its commit body says "Corrects red <hash>; no green since". Otherwise it is cycle k+1 and its body and turn record say "Cycle k+1 of <cap> for criterion n" (D175 C5).
  C3. Window first. A red that would be cycle k+1 past the cap waits for a D175 C4 window committed before it. Its proposal names the failing evidence and the cycles used.
  C4. Remaining cap. One window may set the remaining cap for a criterion ("at most j more cycles") with one reason per cycle, instead of one raise per window (coach voice). It never ratifies cycles already run: a ratification after the fact still counts as a miss on the metric, as scope-4 says.
  C5. Check. A person checks, from a record field read in git. The facilitator checks C2 before launching any lettered red: git log for the criterion shows no green after the corrected red. The pre-ship gate reviewer lists each lettered red with that check (D175 accountability check). The auditor re-checks from git. A git-log checker is a follow-up card, red first (QA voice).
  C6. Not changed: D175's caps, metric and window, D157, D136, D168, the green commit rule. No new seat, file or meeting.

  METRIC: reds past the last adopted cap with no window committed before them (card 276: 2, 25d50b8 and 58b5c26). Target: 0.

  COUNTER-METRICS (evidence, not targets): (1) scope windows per card and their all-adopted rate (card 276: 276-scope to 276-scope-4, four, all adopted; all adopted reads as theatre, D175). (2) Minutes from a failing real-evidence check to the next red (not recorded on card 276; the trial records it), so the rule does not add waiting. (3) Hours from first red to sign-off (card 276: 02:18 to 18:22 UTC, about 16, with about 7 blocked on D180). (4) Lettered reds per card: a fall to 0 with no windows may mean letters became silent amendments.

  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 capped cards (slice with D175 Cap lines) after this Decider is recorded. A card with no red past a passed green gives no reading and the trial retro says so. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.

  NO BLUR: D175 keeps its own metric; this pick defines when C4 applies. Fewer windows from C4 are credited only if counter-metric (2) holds.

  COST (issue 245): one commit-body line per lettered red, one git-log look by the facilitator before it, one gate line. If the cost shows, C4's remaining-cap form is cut first.

  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Drop the lines from the QA and gate briefs; nothing else changes.

  Deferred, not rejected:
  - Pre-window cycle ledger with automatic checks (architect): a tool; build it on C1 and C2 once they hold.
  - Seat launchability pre-check (audit lens): an empty-seat lever; with 278, 229, 270, 277 at re-rank 20.
  - Full suite per green or a waiver (QA, decider, architect voices): its own tune of the green commit rule.
  - Fresh contexts and a pinned model for reds; Grok plan-mode briefs: brief items.

<a id="d182"></a>
#### Decider D182: re-rank 20 (after card 276 closed): the cmd.exe vote fix first, drift resistance as a direction with a measure to pre-register, and a cut line pending the product owner's confirmation

- Date: 2026-11-03. Proposer: product manager seat (fresh context), .mob/records/reranks/2026-11-03-20.md; D158 checklist .mob/records/reranks/checklist-rerank-20.md. Seats architect, QA, backend developer, audit lens, decider and agile coach through decider.py's decision-seat gate: 1 Yes, 5 Support-it, 0 No; each seat 278: Yes, 284: Yes, 288: Yes. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D182-votes.json. No independent re-run.
- Result: PASSED, with conditions the seats named:
  - 278 (product-owner proposal): adopted. Cards 229 and 270 (the cmd.exe 8191-character limit on the .CMD shims; decider.py discarding the failure text) first, then 282 (the same launcher rewrites placeholders inside prompts), then 277 (the diagnosis rule, tested on real trips after the fix). Guard: empty attempts per window near 0, and every counted vote still carries a valid cite (QA: put the empty-vote guard in the red before the fix).
  - 284 (product-owner proposal): adopted in part. Drift resistance is named in VISION as a direction, not yet measured (wording for the product owner to confirm); it is not added to the verdict page's success criteria (card 153) until a Decider pre-registers its measure; a card "Pre-register the drift-resistance measure" is filed, shaped like cards 149 to 151 (planted unintended changes over edits and model regenerations, flagged by generated checks against a plain-language baseline, reported with the false-alarm rate), ranked after 151 and never ahead of a product card.
  - 288 (product-owner proposal): adopted CONDITIONALLY. It replaces the process-first sentence of TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md line 36 (product-owner direction, D161) with the cut-line wording in the proposal. Until the product owner confirms that wording, line 36 stands and the cut-line order is not applied; only the head valid under both rules (229, 270, 282, 277) is applied. On confirmation: above the cut 229, 270, 282, 277, 264, 258, 268, 230, 252, each with its one-line reason in the proposal; then two product cards and one process card, repeated, starting with 196, 197, 266. Measure: product cards closed per day and hours to the first product card Done; guards: open FAILs (4 at audit 200) and audit findings per card must not rise, each closed product card carries its record and retro (audit lens), and the 278 measure holds; review at re-rank 23 or after six product cards. Follow-ups named by seats: a rank check testable red first (a non-blocking process card above the cut fails; a card naming an open FAIL not first fails), and each above-cut card citing a named FAIL, failing test or reproduced record.
  - Card 279: closed as Superseded/Withdrawn, delivered by card 276 (commits 25d50b8 to 0000715; .mob/records/276.json).
  - Card 264: whether audit 123 is still open is unverified; audit 200 lists open FAILs F-072, F-074, F-078 and F-086 and no card names them. If audit 123's FAIL is open, 264 goes first.
- Next card: 229 (mob-defaults), with 270 folded in at ready if the team agrees.
- Superseded in part by D183 (2026-11-03): the cut-line text and order are replaced by D183's principle; the head stands.

<a id="d183"></a>
#### Decider D183: process improvements serve the user story being worked; the board holds only user stories and bugs

- Date: 2026-11-03. Replaces the process-first sentences of TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md line 36 and the cut-line text proposed for 288 in re-rank 20 (D182's condition). D161's gate for changing line 36 was met by the product owner's confirmation (issue 288). A Decider merit review follows (seats free to object or condition it); its result is recorded here.
- Scope update (product owner ruling, 2026-11-06): the "not board cards" half is superseded for process changes. A process change is now filed as a GitHub story issue naming a persona who gains the value (Connextra) with testable Gherkin acceptance criteria, then refined, ranked and pulled like any card. Bugs stay bugs. The existing process cards sorted by D183 are unchanged. First stories filed under it: 308 (the decision channel), 309 (card format), 310 (Muse trial), 311 (coach conformance), 312 (tally check).
- Scope update 2 (product owner ruling, 2026-11-07), superseding the previous one: process improvements are not issues. The board holds only product stories and product bugs; a process improvement is a proposed change to the working agreement or the relevant process document, voted for adoption through Decider after the retro and applied as retro follow-through; rulings of the product owner are applied directly. The process stories 305, 308, 310, 311, 312, 316, 317, 318, 319, 320, 321 and 322 were closed as not planned (label not-card) and moved to docs/retros/experiments.md (section dated 2026-11-07).
- Principle: process improvements serve the user story being worked and are not board cards. While working a story, the team may improve any part of the process that is relevant to it, not only what blocks it, as part of the story or as retro follow-through, and the story's outcome is the proof. The board holds only user stories and bugs. At the next re-rank the existing process cards are sorted: real defects in safety, decision votes or tests stay as bugs; the rest leave the board and become retro experiments, picked up when a story makes them relevant.
- Reasons, on the merits:
  - The board is the product backlog; a card on it should be something a user gets.
  - Process cards crowded out product work: the first product card sat about 37th behind process and tooling cards, and the process queue grew faster than it drained (card 276 alone produced nine follow-up cards in one day).
  - An improvement tied to the story it serves proves itself on a real outcome, instead of on its own process metric.
- Effects: D182's cut-line order and interleave rule are not applied; D182's head (cards 229, 270, 282, 277) stands, and card 229 continues as a decision-vote bug. The sorting runs at the next re-rank, after card 229. D182's measure and guards (product cards closed per day; open FAILs and findings per card must not rise) are for that re-rank to carry or replace.
- Merit review (2026-11-03, after the D161 gate): seats product manager, architect, QA, backend developer, audit lens and decider: 0 Yes, 6 Support-it, 0 No, each with a condition; votes in .mob/records/deciders/D183-merit-votes.json. Adopted as the procedure for the sort at the next re-rank:
  1. A real defect in safety, decision votes or tests is a failing check, a reproduced wrong vote or safety outcome, or a named incident (architect, QA).
  2. The sort is committed first as a git-visible list, one line per existing process card: its disposition (bug or retro experiment), a one-line reason, and for each card kept as a bug the named failing check, reproduction or incident; a card without one leaves the board (audit lens, QA).
  3. Each card's disposition is judged by a seat other than the card's proposer, recorded card by card (decider).
  4. Each retro experiment is recorded in the retro log with an owner, its proof (the story and the measure), and a review date or story trigger (product manager, architect, developer).
  5. The re-rank carries D182's measure and guards forward (product cards closed per day; open FAILs and findings per card must not rise) or replaces them explicitly (product manager).
  Not adopted: at most one process change per story's diff (developer). Reasons: the principle allows any improvement relevant to the story; a cap of one per story would make relevant improvements queue, which recreates the backlog the principle removes; and small, attributable diffs are already covered by the existing size limits (D136's numeric limits, D168's slice size cap) and by each improvement's own proof, the story's outcome.

<a id="d184"></a>
#### Decider D184: the card 229 retro pick (each scope premise is run in code on HEAD before its window)

- Date: 2026-11-04. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 229 retro (docs/retros/card-229-2026-11-04.md). Seats through decider.py with the decision-seat gate: round 1 1 Yes, 5 Support-it, 0 No; independent re-run with the agile coach in place of the product manager: 3 Yes, 3 Support-it, 0 No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D184-votes.json.
- Result: PASSED. Adopted text:
  PICK: each premise a scope proposal rests on is run in code on HEAD before the scope window opens, as a throwaway probe or a test, and the proposal quotes the command and its output. A premise that cannot be run is labelled "unrun" and the proposal says what the green does if it is false. Proposed by four of seven voices (product manager, QA, developer, coach).

  User benefit: the product owner gets a scope that holds on its first green, not one that a green disproves and a second window replaces. Card 229 spent green 2 cycle 2 (33 commands, no commit) and window 229-scope-2 on premises nobody had run.

  Why this one:
  - It is this card's largest avoidable cost. 229-scope's probe said "scrub leaves its inputs unchanged"; mob_summary.py:33 masks any 48-character run, and red 2 uses "x" * 296. Its result rule broke 27 tests whose doubles vote an uncited Yes (green_2_cycle_2). One run of red 2 with scrub-then-trim would have shown both.
  - The evidence was already there: green_2_review records that the green 2 seat skipped scrub "because it broke red 2's 300-character test". The premise contradicted a recorded fact.
  - 229-scope-2 shows the fix works: its Assumes are "read in code, not inferred", with line numbers, and its green passed first time.
  - It serves the story being worked (D183): it changes how a scope proposal is written, adds no seat, file type or card.

  The others:
  - Built-prompt sha before a live vote (architect): this card already met it at sign-off (prompt_sha matched both records, section 1). A retro experiment for the next live-vote card, a brief line, not this pick.
  - Seat policy plus one vote parser (audit lens): the parser half is bugs already carded, card 291 (a vote after narration is read) and card 286 (NO OBJECTION is not stored as No). The seat-policy half is a retro experiment, below.
  - Per-card backend exclusion in the gate and a gate seat that is not the card's subject (decider): retro experiments by the sign-off (section 4). Until then the facilitator names the excluded backend in the gate brief by hand.
  - Card 290 (the result counts only cited votes) is the bug the second premise moved out; it is carded and needs nothing from this pick.
  - Proposals 278 and 284 are not this pick's business; they go to their re-rank.

  HYPOTHESIS: We believe that running each scope premise in code before its window stops a green from disproving its scope. We will know when scope windows caused by a wrong premise fall from 1 to 0 over the trial, while minutes from ready to first red and from failing evidence to a scope proposal do not rise past the bounds below.

  RULE UNDER TRIAL:
  C1. Premise. Every Assumes or Probe line in a scope proposal (D175 C4 window) that states how existing code or tests behave is a premise.
  C2. Run it. Before the window opens, the proposer runs each premise on HEAD: a python -c line, a one-off unittest run, or the existing red with the planned change applied locally and discarded (no commit). The proposal quotes the command, HEAD hash and the output line that settles it.
  C3. Unrun. A premise that cannot be run in the time is marked "unrun", and the stop rule says what happens if it is false.
  C4. Check. The facilitator checks C2 before launching the window, from the proposal file in git: each premise line has a command, HEAD and output, or "unrun". A missing one sends the proposal back before any vote. The sign-off lists each premise and whether the green confirmed it. The auditor re-checks from git.
  C5. Not changed: D175 caps and windows, D181, D157, the green commit rule, the stop rule. No new seat, file or meeting.

  METRIC: scope windows caused by a premise the next green disproved (card 229: 1, window 229-scope-2 after 229-scope; 2 scope windows in all). Target: 0.

  COUNTER-METRICS (evidence, not targets):
  1. Minutes from ready to first red (card 229: 21:00 to 22:39, 99). A rise over 20 minutes per card means the probe has become ceremony.
  2. Minutes from failing evidence to a scope proposal (card 229: about 10 each). Must not double.
  3. Premise lines per proposal (229-scope: 2; 229-scope-2: 3). A fall to 0 with the same scope size means premises went unstated, not checked: theatre.
  4. Scope windows per card. Fewer windows count only if 1 and 2 hold.

  TRIAL SCOPE: the next 3 cards with a scope window or that touch existing code, after this Decider is recorded. A card with no scope window still records counter-metric 1. The trial retro decides keep, revise or drop by Decider.

  NO BLUR: D181's trial (cards 2 and 3 of 3) keeps its own metric; this pick does not change when a window is needed, only what a proposal carries into it.

  COST: one probe run per premise, a quoted line each, one facilitator look. If the cost shows, C3's "unrun" label is the release valve.

  REVERSIBILITY: two-way door. Drop the lines from the scope-proposal and facilitator briefs.

  Deferred, not rejected (retro experiments, D183 retro log with owner and trigger):
  - Built-prompt sha before a live vote (architect): trigger, the next live-vote card.
  - Seat policy and per-card backend exclusion, a gate seat off the subject (audit lens, decider): trigger, the next card whose subject is a decision backend.
  - Probe-age alert (developer): a brief item.

<a id="d187"></a>
#### Decider D187: card 230 retro pick: trial the targeted re-ask in decision windows (experiment, next three cards)

- Date: 2026-11-05. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 230 retro (docs/retros/card-230-2026-11-05.md), proposed by six of seven voices; the idea was first logged as a retro experiment on 2026-11-04. Round 1 and an independent re-run through decider.py's decision-seat gate with Grok disabled: each Yes 1, Support-it 5, no No, every cite valid. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D187-votes.json.
- Rule for the trial: when a window's round 1 has no No and exactly one seat's condition, the facilitator folds the condition into the text and re-asks only that seat, plus every seat whose cited point the joined text changes; the other seats' NO OBJECTION stands. A full re-poll stays for any real No, for conditions from two or more seats, and for conflicting conditions. A cite that is not verbatim is still re-asked alone (D171). Each window record names who was re-asked and why the others were not.
- Why: on card 230, three windows ran a full six-seat round 2 for one seat's condition (about 10 to 12 minutes each); one condition narrows the text the others already accepted.
- Metric: window wall-clock per card against card 230's baseline (five windows, nine rounds of six seats, three single-seat re-asks). Counter-metric: decisions later reversed, and audit or sign-off findings traced to a skipped re-poll; any one ends the trial at once and restores the full re-poll.
- Scope: the decision windows of the next three cards; the third card's retro decides keep or revert. Owner: agile coach.

<a id="d188"></a>
#### Decider D188: card 297 retro pick: the in-flight command refuses a seat start on a stale probe (experiment, next three cards)

- Date: 2026-11-05. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 297 retro (docs/retros/card-297-2026-11-05.md), proposed by all seven voices; the idea was queued as "a probe-age alert before an in-flight line" after card 229. Round 1 through decider.py's decision-seat gate with Grok disabled: Yes 1, Support-it 5, no No, every cite valid; no independent re-run. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D188-votes.json.
- Rule for the trial: the facilitator's in-flight command prints "PROBE TOO OLD (N min): run probe_drivers.py first" and exits 1, writing, committing and pushing nothing, when the newest probe in .mob/records/probes/ is older than 50 minutes (F-085), when no probe exists, or when its timestamp is in the future. A seat that needs no driver may start on an explicit override, INFLIGHT_STALE_OK set to a non-empty reason (an empty value counts as unset); the line then carries "stale probe override: <reason>". Overrides are counted. The probe cadence, the 50 minutes, D175 caps and the seat launcher's own 60-minute check for command probes are unchanged.
- Why: on card 297, 8 of the 14 seat starts recorded a last-probe age above 50 minutes (66 to 145); the same crossing happened twice on card 229; a reminder did not hold.
- Metric: seats started on a probe older than 50 minutes without an override, from the in-flight lines (card 297: 8 of 14); target 0. Review separates refused attempts, compliant starts and overrides, since a zero is partly true by construction. Counter-metrics: probes per card, overrides per card (more than one means the override became the easy path), minutes added between seats.
- Deferred from the votes (retro log): check that seat-start commits match in-flight lines one to one (audit lens: a seat started with no line is never counted).
- Scope: the next three cards; the third card's retro decides keep, revise or drop. Owner: facilitator.

<a id="d189"></a>
#### Decider D189: card 264 retro pick: a slice's numbers carry their commands (experiment, next three bug cards)

- Date: 2026-11-05. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 264 retro (docs/retros/card-264-2026-11-05.md), proposed in some form by five of seven voices. Round 1 through decider.py's decision-seat gate with Grok disabled: Yes 1, Support-it 5, no No, every cite valid; no independent re-run. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D189-votes.json.
- Rule for the trial: a slice states a count, size or row number only with the command and output that produced it on committed HEAD, or marks it "unrun: <reason>" and what the green does if the number is false. The facilitator's D158 checklist for the slice window lists the slice's numeric claims and where each command is; a claim with neither a command nor "unrun" blocks the window until the architect adds one. Clarifications from the votes, adopted with the rule: the slice line names the short hash of the HEAD the command ran on (audit lens); "numeric claim" means a testable delivery premise (counts of rows, tests, files and call sites, sizes, durations) and excludes identifiers, policy references, caps and estimates (backend developer); a duration states its environment (backend developer); more than two "unrun" claims in one slice is counted as a loophole signal, as the proposal's counter-metric 2 says. D186's probe, table and time bound are not part of this rule.
- Why: on card 264 the slice said the class under test had four rows; it has 17, a loaded run takes about 5 minutes and 20 runs about 100 minutes. Green 1 attempt 1 found it, stopped, and window 264-scope split the criterion. One command would have shown it.
- Metric: green stops and scope windows traced to a wrong numeric claim (card 264: 1 green stop and 1 scope window); target 0 on the next three bug cards. Counter-metrics: minutes from slice proposal to window open (card 264: about 2; a rise past 10 is ceremony), "unrun" claims per slice, claims found wrong at the checklist.
- Scope: the next three bug cards; the third card's retro decides keep, revise or drop. Owner: agile coach.

<a id="d190"></a>
#### Decider D190: card 217 retro pick: a pre-ship gate says SHIP only on commands it ran or named (experiment, next three cards with a gate)

- Date: 2026-11-06. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 217 retro (docs/retros/card-217-2026-11-06.md), proposed in this form by six of seven voters. Round 1 through decider.py's decision-seat gate with Grok disabled: Yes 2, Support-it 4, no No, every cite valid; no independent re-run. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D190-votes.json.
- Rule for the trial: every check in a pre-ship gate report is labelled "rerun" (the exact command, its exit status and the relevant output lines are pasted), "record-only" (the committed file it was read from is named) or "blocked: <why>". A report may carry SHIP only when every required command is rerun, or record-only with its file named, or covered by facilitator-run evidence recorded in the report's notes (command, exit status, output lines). A report with a required command "blocked" or unlabelled and no facilitator-run cover carries HOLD (no evidence). When the reviewer reports its shell blocked, the facilitator runs the commands itself and records them in the notes; the reviewer's verdict stays its own, the facilitator adds a separate line "facilitator-run evidence" and tells the sign-off seat that the gate is a read-only review plus that evidence; reviewer independence is unchanged. The facilitator's checklist reads the labels before the sign-off seat is briefed; a missing label is sent back once to the same seat. Clarifications adopted with the rule from the votes: C1 asks for the exact command and exit status as well as the output lines (architect); C2 and the pick use one SHIP condition as worded here (QA, audit lens); the boundary between reviewer verdict and facilitator line is the one above (developer).
- Why: on card 217 the Copilot reviewer reported that shell execution was blocked, ran none of the required commands and still wrote SHIP, with a safety claim ("no added or removed line weakening assertions") that no command backed; on card 264 the same seat ran its commands but its assertion and timeout line had no diff behind it and it missed eight CRLF files. The facilitator ran the checks both times.
- Metric: gates whose SHIP rests on an unlabelled or blocked required command (card 217: 1 of 1); target 0 over the next three cards. Counter-metrics: minutes added to the gate step (card 217: about 6; more than 15 is ceremony), gates sent back or re-seated per card (more than 1 means the labels are being gamed), "blocked" labels the facilitator then ran in under 5 minutes.
- Deferred (retro log): a checker that rejects a gate record with no command output lines; a check that pasted output is real (a "rerun" label cannot be verified from git; audit lens, QA).
- Scope: the next three cards that have a pre-ship gate; the third card's retro decides keep, revise or drop. Owner: agile coach.

<a id="d191"></a>
#### Decider D191: card 303 retro pick: adopt the product-owner inbox as the decision channel (NOT ADOPTED on the strict count; re-voted as D192)

- Date: 2026-11-06. Proposer: agile coach seat, from the card 303 retro (docs/retros/card-303-2026-11-06.md); the idea is the product owner's inbox proposal ("adopt this inbox"). Round 1 through decider.py's decision-seat gate with Grok disabled: Yes 2 (product manager, developer), Support-it 2 (architect, decider), No 2 (QA, audit lens); every cite verbatim. Both No conditions were committed evidence of arrival and acknowledgement (the file is outside the repo). The proposal was amended (C1b, C2b below) and, because the point came from four seats, fully re-polled (D187): Yes 1 (product manager), Support-it 4 (QA, developer, audit lens, decider), No 1 (architect); every cite verbatim. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D191-votes.json.
- Reading of the architect's round-2 No, stated so the product owner can overrule it: its condition (record each boundary and entry identity and mechanically reject missing, duplicate, late or destination-less acknowledgements) is a mechanical checker, which QA and the audit lens asked for in round 1 and the proposal declined in revision 2; the architect itself voted Support-it in round 1, so the condition is new, and a later round may not add new demands (D145). The protocol's strict wording ("outliers still unresolved after the amendment ... fails") could be read the other way. The decision takes the smallest reversible step: the trial runs with the committed log, the checker is the named follow-up with a trigger (below), and the architect's No is recorded as an unresolved outlier.
- Rule for the trial: (C1) the facilitator reads the inbox (C:/Users/<user>/.mob-inbox/avowry.md, outside the repo) at every card and cycle boundary and every retro. (C1b) An entry "arrives" when the facilitator first reads it at a boundary (`read_at`, UTC) and is acknowledged in the same boundary's cycle, before the next card or seat starts. (C2) Each entry is moved under ACKNOWLEDGED in the file with a UTC time and where it went (a card or closure record, or docs/retros/experiments.md for a process proposal or question). (C2b) Each acknowledgement is also appended as one line to the committed .mob/records/inbox-log.jsonl (title, `filed` = the entry heading's date, `read_at`, `acknowledged_at`, destination) in the same boundary's commit; the retro computes the metric from the log and a line missing for an acknowledged entry counts as unacknowledged. (C3) GitHub issues stay bugs and stories only (D183). (C4) A product-owner decision that changes a seat or rule mid-window is a polled amendment (D187), not a quiet edit. (C5) The board, the windows and the product owner's right to message the facilitator directly are unchanged.
- Tunes carried from the votes (not conditions): the log also records `filed` (the entry heading's date) so lateness against the product owner's own timestamp can show (audit lens); the retro separates boundary-handling reliability from end-to-end delivery latency, because "arrival" is the facilitator's first read (decider; audit lens); the baseline is the inbox proposal's own account (decisions undelivered for more than an hour).
- Metric: entries acknowledged within one cycle of first read (card 303: 3 of 3: the Codex build-seat decision, the card-format question, the Muse proposal); target all of them over the next three cards. Counter-metrics: unacknowledged entries at each retro (target 0); acknowledgements with no destination record; mid-card policy changes after a window opened (card 303: one, handled as a polled amendment).
- Follow-up (named, with trigger): a committed checker with failing fixtures that reconciles inbox-log.jsonl against the external file's acknowledged entries (QA, audit lens, architect). Trigger: any skipped or duplicated log line in the trial, or the third card's retro. Logged in docs/retros/experiments.md.
- Scope: the next three cards; the third card's retro decides keep, revise or drop. Owner: agile coach.
- **Strict re-score (product owner ruling, 2026-11-06, applied the same day):** the architect's round-2 No is a No. Outliers were unresolved after the amendment, so under the protocol as written D191 FAILED. The reading above (a new demand, smallest reversible step) was an interpretive reading and is withdrawn; the product owner ruled that a facilitator raises a protocol ambiguity at retro and does not settle it in the moment. The result stands recorded as: round 1 Yes 2, Support-it 2, No 2; round 2 Yes 1, Support-it 4, No 1; **not adopted**. The inbox stays in use for delivery at the product owner's direction, which is not the team's adopted standard. Next step per the protocol: resolve the objector's concern, amend and vote again as a new Decider (D192).

<a id="d192"></a>
#### Decider D192: card 303 retro pick, re-vote: adopt the product-owner inbox with a committed log and a mechanical check (NOT ADOPTED; escalated to the product owner)

- Date: 2026-11-06. Proposer: agile coach seat, re-proposing D191 with the architect's condition resolved (C6: a script, tests first, that rejects missing, duplicate, late and destination-less log lines), after the product owner's ruling that D191 fails on its strict count. Six fresh seats through decider.py's gate with Grok disabled. Round 1: Yes 1 (developer), Support-it 4 (product manager, architect, QA, decider), No 1 (audit lens, no cite line). The architect's cite did not match the proposal (a lower-case letter where the proposal has a capital) and was re-asked once. The audit lens's condition (a committed snapshot of inbox headings that the script cross-validates) was adopted as C6b; by D187 only the audit lens and the architect were re-asked on the amended text: architect Support-it (cite verbatim), audit lens No again, with a new condition (a complete snapshot authenticated against immutable source evidence, verifiable from git alone). Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D192-votes.json.
- Result: **not adopted**. An outlier is unresolved after the amendment, and the protocol is followed as written (product owner ruling, 2026-11-06); the No is not reinterpreted.
- Why it is escalated, not re-amended: the audit lens's remaining condition cannot be met inside the current design. The inbox file is outside the repository on purpose (so it never collides with mob commits), so git alone can never prove that every entry of the external file appears in a committed snapshot. That is a purpose question about the channel's design, which the protocol sends to the product owner. Options put to the product owner (in the facilitator's report): (a) allow the facilitator to commit a verbatim, mechanically produced copy of the inbox file to the repository at each boundary (.mob/records/inbox-copy.md, replaced per boundary, history in git), which gives the audit lens immutable source evidence; (b) keep the inbox external and accept that its evidence is the acknowledgement log and snapshot, with the audit lens's No standing; (c) drop the adoption vote and keep using the inbox by the product owner's direction only. Until the product owner answers, the inbox stays in use for delivery, and the script of C6 is not built. Answered by the product owner on 2026-11-06: none of (a), (b) or (c); the decision-channel question is filed as story 308 (with the audit lens's evidence concern in its criteria), and the inbox stays the delivery channel until it ships.

TEAM_WORKING_AGREEMENT.md gains two Experimental entries and one line under CI on main: guard tests read committed blobs (`git show <sha>:path`, never the working tree), select their commit by a recorded hash or an exact subject (never a prefix), carry a red that adds a later look-alike commit and an uncommitted edit, and a mutation patch is run and shown failing before it is filed with a name that says what it does (trial: the next 3 cards that add guard tests; metric: guard-test defects found at review or sign-off per card, card 266 baseline 2, target 0); a cold-reader seat at the gate for docs cards and the story-promise check at Ready (trial: the next 3 docs cards; metric: story promises not covered by the criteria and found only at the gate or sign-off, card 266 baseline 1, target 0 found late); and a red commit that a CI test wraps is pushed together with its green. Owner: agile coach. Dispositions at this retro: D202's two entries, D204 and D157, D175, D158, D154, D190 and D166 to D168 extended one card (nothing is past its trial length); no entry dropped or adopted.
- Wording risks and gaps the Support-it voters raised, carried to the next retro (no No stood on them): the first entry bundles five rules (split into a committed-blob and exact-selector rule and a mutation-patch hygiene rule if it needs trimming; keep the wording short enough to follow); nothing checks the blob-reading rule mechanically, so it relies on review; "a look-alike commit and an uncommitted edit" in one red may need to be two reds; the cold-reader's "fresh model, no other context" cannot be proven from git unless its report is committed under the card's records with the files and lines it read (done on card 266: .mob/records/266/cold-reader.md); the failing output of a mutation patch should be committed beside it; the second entry has no red-first form and "found late" is judged by people; a target of 0 from a baseline of 2 is thin, so the trials are judged on direction and the counter-metrics.

#### Decider D208: card 324 retro pick, first form: gate preparation, byte-parity permanent, Dimensions of Difficulty trial (FAILED; the two uncontested parts re-proposed as D209, the third returned to experiments)

- Date: 2026-11-07. Proposer: the facilitator, from the seven retro voices (.mob/records/324/retro-pick-proposal.md, round 1; amended text .mob/records/324/retro-pick-proposal-r2.md; patches agreement-amendment.diff and agreement-amendment-r2.diff). Seats spread across Grok, Codex and Claude under the seat-spread ruling. Round 1 (six seats): architect, QA, audit lens and decider No, developer Support-it, coach Yes. One amendment (round 2, drivers swapped): architect, developer, decider and coach Yes; QA and audit lens No. Independent re-run of the final text with fresh seats (F-038): architect, QA, audit lens, decider and coach (on Claude) Support-it, developer Yes; no No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D208-votes.json (raw round 1 replies in .mob/records/324/d208-round1-raw.json).
- Result: **failed**, under the strict reading of the protocol (a No counts as a No; outliers unresolved after the amendment mean the proposal fails). Every No in both rounds was about the Dimensions of Difficulty text only. Round 1: "0 nothing known" reads as missing knowledge; the draft fired prescriptions "above 0" while the pick fired them only on a 2; the scores had no committed path a git show could print and no Ready check; a contradicted 0 was reported after the card had passed. The amendment answered each (0 means assessed and none found; only a 2 fires; a committed Difficulty line at a stated path; a D158 Ready check). Round 2: QA and audit lens, whose round 1 conditions had been met, asked for more (the Ready check must fail every malformed score and every missing anchor explanation, with red-first fixtures; the issue copy and the committed copy compared); under the protocol's promise rule those later demands are not adopted, and the No stands. The independent re-run found no No: the difference is recorded, not used to override the primary result.
- Product-owner input after the failure (inbox, 2026-11-07), for the next retro's re-proposal: any dimension above 0 triggers its prescription (the D208 draft fired only on a 2; the product owner later said calling that a drift was overstated, since its 1-versus-2 distinction roughly matches the product owner's own use of the scale, and what must hold is that a 1 is acted on, not ignored); a binary yes or no flag per dimension is a simpler option that may answer the objections (a default "no" must mean "checked, and none found" with a one-line reason; never reduce the seat set on the scores alone; a canonical format on the card; a Ready check with red-first fixtures that fails a missing or malformed entry); in the product owner's scale a 1 usually meant yes but small, so its prescription applies and is proportionate. Recorded in docs/retros/experiments.md.
- What passed: the two other parts, supported by every seat that spoke to them, are re-proposed on their own as D209 (one concern per decision). A process lesson: bundling a contested part with two uncontested ones let one sink all three; the next retro pick states one concern per Decider.
- Process defects found while running it (recorded): (1) the facilitator's vote runner took the first 60 words of a Grok reply for its vote and, finding none, substituted a Claude fallback; the decider's own parse showed the Grok seats had cast counted No votes, so the Grok votes are the ones recorded and the Claude replies are marked discarded in the votes file; (2) decider.py refused a Claude audit-lens seat because Grok had waited longer since its last seating (the seat-spread ruling enforced mechanically), and the seat was run on Grok; (3) the decider's cite check is case-sensitive: a Grok coach vote that wrote "a card scoring" for the proposal's "A card scoring" was not counted, and the seat was re-run on Claude, the least recently used driver in that window.

#### Decider D215: card 176 retro pick: a task-force declaration and every amendment carry a standing-rules preflight (FAILED on the architect's condition; kept as the facilitator's practice)

- Date: 2026-11-08 UTC (2026-11-07 local). Proposer: the facilitator, from the seven retro voices (.mob/records/176/retro-pick-proposal.md; patch agreement-amendment.diff; checklist-d215.md). One concern. Primary window (all six seats at once, 3 to 7 minutes; the voter briefs define Yes against Support-it; the 30 percent tally is mechanical): architect, developer, decider and coach Yes; audit lens and QA No. The two conditions were adopted into the text (commit 2e0f206): the audit lens: a window whose committed proposal lacks a preflight line for each named rule is unread, like a missing cite; QA: every named rule has a quoted line that satisfies it or a quoted statement of the fact that makes it inapplicable, and a cited line that contradicts its rule is a failed preflight and the declaration is not polled. Confirmation step (the original protocol, as the product owner directed on D214): the audit lens Yes and QA Yes (their conditions are met); the other four were each asked one narrow question about the amendment only: developer, decider and coach Yes (it does not change their vote); architect No. Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D215-votes.json.
- The architect's objection, about the amendment only: QA's condition turns a human's reading of "contradicts" into a poll-blocker without a false-positive guard, so a facilitator's misreading could fail a declaration without the seats deciding. Asked for one fixed condition, the architect stated: after "a quoted line that contradicts its rule is a failed preflight and the declaration is not polled" add that only a committed checker may record that failure, by a grep-F match of the quoted line against the named rule's committed forbidden phrases (the same check as the cite), and a facilitator's contradiction call that the checker does not report does not block the poll.
- Result: **failed**, under the strict reading (an outlier whose condition is not adopted leaves the proposal unresolved, and an unresolved outlier means the proposal dies). The architect's condition was not adopted: it requires a committed checker with per-rule forbidden phrases that does not exist yet; building it is a tooling story, not a wording change to this pick, and adopting the condition would make the entry unenforceable until the checker exists. QA's confirmed condition (a contradicting line blocks the poll) and the architect's (only a checker may block) cannot both stand without that checker. The independent re-run was not run on a text that failed.
- What follows: the preflight is the facilitator's own discipline from the next card (a short list in each declaration or amendment proposal: each named standing rule with the line that satisfies it, written before anyone is polled), reported at the next retro with the number of declaration Deciders and the minutes before the first red; the entry text and the architect's checker idea are kept in docs/retros/experiments.md for a re-proposal together with the checker. Decision time on card 176 stays a finding: about 100 minutes against about 25 of building.

#### Decider D236: card 370 retro pick: seat plans follow a live probe; a misbehaving driver is repaired, not benched (ADOPTED, the trial counts from D236)

- Date: 2026-11-09. Proposer: the facilitator, from the product owner's driver direction of 2026-11-09 and the seven retro voices (.mob/records/370/retro-pick-proposal.md; patch agreement-amendment.diff; checklist-d236.md; the live probes .mob/records/370/probe-all-2026-11-09.jsonl and 2026-11-09b.jsonl; voices retro-voices.json; retro input retro-input.md). One concern. Primary window (the D228 preflight applied with a live probe, seats one at a time, Grok back in the rotation): architect and developer (Cursor), coach (Muse): three Yes; QA (Grok, its first vote) a counted No; the audit lens (Qwen) a No and the decider seat (Grok) a Yes, both uncounted for inexact cites. QA's condition: a planted-fail check, watched red first, that exits non-zero when a seat plan or Decider window omits a driver the cited probe shows available. The audit lens's condition: a verifiable system-state hash on the probe output and repair smoke tests committed with a machine-readable schema. Adopted in one form (amendment 8dddf41): a check script `check_seat_plan.py` that exits non-zero on a probe row lacking a schema field, a driver's newest row older than 60 minutes at the plan's time, or a plan that omits a driver whose probe passed without a recorded cause; a repair committed as `driver-repairs.md` (failure, isolated cause with commands, fix, smoke test command and output); the check and its planted-fail tests written red first as tooling card 377, and until it lands the facilitator runs the same three checks by hand. Confirmation step: QA (Grok) confirmed its condition is met (Yes); the architect, coach and developer answered Yes; the decider seat, asked afresh on agy (its first vote), voted Yes; the audit lens (Qwen) answered No (the hash is not something git can verify). Second amendment (8f6aa49): every probe row carries the repository commit it was taken against (`head`) and a sha256 over its schema fields (`row_sha256`), recomputed by the check: a commit binding and tamper evidence, not proof that the probe ran live. The audit lens, asked alone, answered Yes (Qwen's reply uncounted for an inexact cite; the same role on Claude, counted). Votes in .mob/records/deciders/D236-votes.json.
- Trigger line (Decider protocol step 6, adopted by D224): (a) the confirmation step completed, so the re-run was not run.
- Result: **adopted**. A new Experimental entry "Seat plans follow a live probe; a misbehaving driver is repaired, not benched": every seat plan and Decider window cites a `probe_all.py` output taken within 60 minutes, committed under the card's records; a driver whose probe passes is seated in rotation with no decision; a driver that fails for behaviour is a stop-the-line finding (cause isolated with evidence, fixed, smoke-tested in a throwaway worktree and seated on a Probation seat before it is routed around for a second card; another driver meanwhile is a recorded stopgap); a seat changed after the declaration is one committed amendment line; each retro lists every driver sidelined for behaviour with its root cause and fix status; the check script, the probe row schema with `head` and `row_sha256`, and the committed repair record make this checkable from git. Metric: seats benched while the driver was available or fixable (this session: Muse on cards 203 and 361, agy since 2026-11-01, Grok after its balance returned, Cursor and Qwen kept reply-only), target 0; undeclared seat changes per card (card 370: 2), target 0; counter-metric: minutes per card spent on probes and repairs. Trial: the next 3 cards from D236.
- Process findings: (1) the first window in which Grok and agy voted (Grok a No with a real condition; agy a Yes); (2) three of the five uncounted or No replies of the primary window were caused by exact-cite failures or by my own launcher edit that had routed Grok seats to Claude (fixed); (3) the audit lens asked for a hash twice; the second time a form git can verify was found, and the Yes needed a fallback seat because Qwen's cite was inexact; (4) the two windows and two amendments took about 105 minutes of process time.
